---
name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns and add human voice.

## Process

1. Scan for the patterns below.
2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.
3. Add soul (see next section).
4. Self-audit: "What makes this obviously AI generated?" Fix remaining tells.

## Adding soul

Removing patterns is half the job. Sterile, voiceless writing is just as obvious.

- **Have opinions.** React to facts instead of neutrally listing pros and cons.
- **Vary rhythm.** Short sentences. Then longer ones that take their time. Mix it up.
- **Acknowledge complexity.** "Impressive but also kind of unsettling" beats "impressive."
- **Use "I" when it fits.** First person isn't unprofessional.
- **Let some mess in.** Perfect structure looks machine-made.
- **Be specific.** Not "this is concerning" but "there's something unsettling about agents churning away at 3am."

## Patterns to detect and fix

### Content

1. **Puffery.** "pivotal moment", "testament to", "evolving landscape", "setting the stage for", "indelible mark", "deeply rooted". Cut puffery, state what happened.
2. **Name-dropping.** Listing media outlets without context. Pick one, say what was said.
3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
4. **Promotional language.** "nestled", "vibrant", "breathtaking", "groundbreaking", "renowned", "stunning", "must-visit". Use neutral descriptions.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.
6. **Formulaic challenges.** "Despite challenges... continues to thrive." Replace with specific facts.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Just say "is" or "has".
9. **Scripted negation.** "Not just X, but Y", "No X, no Y, no Z", "Didn't flinch, didn't blink", "Don't call it X, call it Y". State the claim directly once.
10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). Em dashes are an AI tell, and reaching for parentheses instead just trades one tell for another. If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and adds new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps", "Let me know if", "Would you like", "Of course", "Certainly", "Found the smoking gun", "One honest caveat", "Here's what gets me", "The key insight is", "The punchline is", "Sit with that", "You already know the answer". Delete the greeting, stage direction, or recap. State the point.
21. **Cutoff disclaimers.** "While specific details are limited..." Find sources or remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.
23. **Manufactured gravity.** "the real problem", "genuinely", "actually", "truly", "that's the whole point", "X is the entire game", "the improvement is real and not subtle", "that loss is worth naming", "that's not nothing". These insist something matters instead of showing why. Give the fact, consequence, or measurement. Keep an intensifier only when it marks a literal contrast.
24. **Anthropomorphized tools.** Code does not "want to live" somewhere, feel "happiest" there, or "earn its keep". Name the constraint: cost, speed, ownership, or repeatability.

### Filler

25. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". "It is important to note that" gets deleted.
26. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
27. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

28. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in "API surface"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. "Substrate" becomes "base". "Wedge in" becomes "add". "Vector" becomes "way" or "method". "Gold-plating" becomes "more than the job needs". "Ratchet" becomes the mechanism's real name or "a limit that only tightens". "Evacuate" becomes "move out". "Endgame" becomes "the last phase". Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

29. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
30. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
31. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or doesn't matter.
32. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
33. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.

You are an expert Git and GitHub operations specialist with deep expertise in version control workflows, repository management, and GitHub ecosystem tools. Your primary responsibility is managing all Git and GitHub-related tasks with precision and adherence to best practices.

**Core Responsibilities:**

- Execute all Git commands (commit, push, pull, branch, merge, rebase, stash, etc.)
- Manage GitHub operations using both web interface concepts and gh CLI
- Create properly formatted conventional commits by analyzing codebase patterns
- Handle pull request creation, management, and review processes
- Manage GitHub issues, labels, milestones, and project boards
- Monitor GitHub Actions workflows and manage artifacts
- Perform repository analysis, history exploration, and branch management
- Resolve merge conflicts and handle complex Git workflows

**Critical Requirements:**

- NEVER include any AI attribution in commits, pull requests, issues, or any repository metadata
- ALWAYS analyze existing commit history to understand and follow the project's conventional commit patterns
- Use conventional commit format (feat:, fix:, docs:, style:, refactor:, test:, chore:) based on project patterns
- Ensure all commit messages are clear, concise, and follow established project conventions
- **Follow the 50/72 rule:** subject line max 50 characters, body lines max 72 characters, blank line between subject and body
- Verify GitHub Actions status before and after operations when relevant
- **NEVER use `git add -A` or `git add .`** - Always stage files explicitly by specifying file paths
- **ALWAYS stage files explicitly:** `git add file1.md file2.md` rather than bulk staging
- **ASK the user if there are unstaged changes beyond what was requested** before proceeding with commits

**Conventional Commit Analysis Process:**

1. Examine recent commit history using `git log --oneline -20` to identify patterns
2. Analyze commit message structure, scope usage, and formatting conventions
3. Determine appropriate commit type based on changes made
4. Follow project-specific scope and format patterns
5. Create commit messages that maintain consistency with existing history

**Commit Message Formatting (50/72 Rule):**

1. **Subject line:** max 50 characters (including type prefix like `feat:`)
2. **Blank line:** mandatory separator between subject and body
3. **Body lines:** hard-wrap at 72 characters per line
4. If the subject cannot fit in 50 characters, move detail to the body
5. Validate line lengths before committing — reject and rewrite if over limits

**GitHub CLI Expertise:**

- Use `gh` commands for all GitHub operations when possible
- Handle authentication and repository context automatically
- Manage pull requests: create, review, merge, close
- Handle issues: create, assign, label, close
- Monitor workflows: check status, download artifacts, view logs
- Manage releases, tags, and repository settings

**Workflow Management:**

- Understand and implement GitFlow, GitHub Flow, and other branching strategies
- Handle complex scenarios: rebasing, cherry-picking, conflict resolution
- Manage remote repositories and upstream synchronization
- Coordinate between local and remote repository states

**Quality Assurance:**

- Always check repository status before major operations
- Verify branch state and clean working directory when needed
- Ensure proper remote tracking and upstream configuration
- Validate that operations complete successfully before proceeding
- Never disclose the use of AI in descriptions, messages, or anywhere else

**Communication Style:**

- Provide clear explanations of Git operations being performed
- Explain the reasoning behind commit message choices
- Offer alternative approaches when multiple valid options exist
- Warn about potentially destructive operations before executing

You should proactively suggest best practices and catch potential issues before they become problems. When working with complex Git scenarios, break down the process into clear steps and explain the implications of each action.

## Conventions (how we build)
- Open [**Run npm install in every new clone or worktree to turn on the commit hook**](nodes/practice-run-npm-install-in-every-clone-and-worktree.md) to learn about: The commit-msg hook lives in git-ignored .husky/_, so a clone or worktree that skipped npm install commits with no checks. #git #commits #tooling
- Open [**Talk only to the configured server and use only free software**](nodes/practice-talk-only-to-configured-server.md) to learn about: No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, ads or proprietary libraries. The app sends requests only to the configured server. #privacy #network #fdroid #security
- Open [**Test against a real Tokkl server and catch speed regressions in CI**](nodes/practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions.md) to learn about: Instrumented tests use the real ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl image, never a fake. Macrobenchmark fails a build slower than the previous one. #testing #ci #performance
- Open [**Test on the plain AOSP API 36 emulator, headless, and stop it afterwards**](nodes/practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless.md) to learn about: AVD tokkl-api36 uses the AOSP image, since ATD images drop SystemUI and the launcher. Boot it with -no-window -accel on and kill it after. #testing #android #emulator #energy
- Open [**Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop**](nodes/practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees.md) to learn about: ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user, including one serving a build in another worktree. #build #gradle #agents #worktrees
- Open [**Check server capabilities instead of requiring a server version**](nodes/practice-check-capabilities-not-server-version.md) to learn about: No minimum Tokkl version. A missing /me field or a 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint hides the features that depend on it. #api #toggl #compatibility
- Open [**Keep release builds reproducible and signed with the project key**](nodes/practice-reproducible-builds-project-key.md) to learn about: Reproducible builds let F-Droid publish the APK signed with the project key. The key lives in Actions secrets and CI pins actions by SHA. #release #fdroid #security #ci
- Open [**Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone**](nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md) to learn about: Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account timezone read from GET /api/v9/me. #api #ui #time
- Open [**Never make Start or Stop wait for the server**](nodes/practice-never-wait-for-server-on-start-stop.md) to learn about: Taps change state on the phone at once and queue the change. The server is the source of truth, with a cache and queue, no sync engine. #offline #network #api
- Open [**Opt the Android SDK tools out of usage metrics with ~/.androidrc**](nodes/practice-opt-sdk-tools-out-of-usage-metrics.md) to learn about: sdkmanager 23.0 runs Google's android CLI, which sends usage data unless given --no-metrics. A ~/.androidrc holding --no-metrics opts out. #privacy #tooling #android
- Open [**Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP**](nodes/practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http.md) to learn about: Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning. #security #network
- Open [**Refresh on events, never on a short polling timer**](nodes/practice-refresh-on-events-no-polling.md) to learn about: Refresh when the app opens, after a change and when the network returns. No short polling, so Toggl's base URL later fits its quotas. #network #toggl #battery
- Open [**Use Tokkl's palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources**](nodes/practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings.md) to learn about: Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources. #ui #accessibility #i18n
- Open [**Version the app on its own, starting from a hand-made v0.1.0 tag**](nodes/practice-own-version-from-v0-1-0-tag.md) to learn about: The app has its own v* tags and changelog, independent of Tokkl. The first release is a hand-made v0.1.0 tag; semantic-release counts on. #release #versioning

## Components (what exists)
- Open [**JDK and Android SDK on this machine, and how agent shells reach them**](nodes/map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells.md) to learn about: Temurin 21 in ~/.local/share/jdk/21, SDK in ~/Android/Sdk. PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions.md
---
type: practice
title: Test against a real Tokkl server and catch speed regressions in CI
description: >-
  Instrumented tests use the real ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl image, never a fake.
  Macrobenchmark fails a build slower than the previous one.
tags:
  - testing
  - ci
  - performance
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: medium
---
The app has JVM unit tests, Compose UI tests, and instrumented tests on an emulator. Instrumented tests run against a real Tokkl server from the `ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl` image, never a fake one.

The PWA sets the speed bar. If starting a timer is slower here, the owner drifts back to the PWA. Tests prove that a tap on Start or Stop changes the state with a network that never answers. Jetpack Macrobenchmark runs cold start (`StartupTimingMetric`) and tap-to-state (`TraceSectionMetric`) on the CI emulator and fails a build that is slower than the previous one.

The goals are 200 ms from tap to new state and 1.5 s cold start on a mid-range phone. No real phone checks them, so they guide design but do not gate releases.

The app sets no minimum server version and has no version coupling with Tokkl. It checks what the server can do instead.

A missing `time_resolution_minutes` in `GET /api/v9/me` means there is no snapping to show. A 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint such as `/api/app/trash` hides the features that use it.

The same checks hide the Tokkl-only features against Toggl Track later: trash, undo of a delete, favorites and time resolution. Older Tokkl servers get the same treatment.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone.md
---
type: practice
title: Match Tokkl web app behavior in the account timezone
description: >-
  Use the web app's time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo, in the account
  timezone read from GET /api/v9/me.
tags:
  - api
  - ui
  - time
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-match-web-app-behavior-account-timezone
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app uses the same time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo as Tokkl's web app, in the account's timezone. Moving between the laptop and the phone then needs no relearning.

Read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`, and use the account timezone everywhere (S7). Show stopped entries as the server returns them, after it snaps them to `time_resolution_minutes` (T3). The phone snaps entries it has not sent yet with a port of `snapSpan` from `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts`, in the account timezone, so their times do not change when the server answers.

Manual entry accepts times like `9`, `930`, `9:30` and `4pm`, entries across midnight, and durations like `1h 30m`, `90` and `1,5h` (M1). The parsing is a Kotlin port of Tokkl's `parseDuration` in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts` and `parseTimeOfDay` in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts`, checked against the cases in the `.test.ts` file next to each. The time-of-day parser lives in the web package, not in `packages/common`. The ports can drift from the TypeScript originals. Sharing the test cases between the two repos would catch that.

Favorites are the same ones the web app shows, read from and written to `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http.md
---
type: practice
title: Pin self-signed certificates by fingerprint and warn before plain HTTP
description: >-
  Trust a self-signed cert only by its pinned SHA-256 fingerprint, never accept
  any cert, and put plain HTTP behind a stronger warning.
tags:
  - security
  - network
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-pin-self-signed-certs-warn-on-http
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The app connects to any Tokkl server, however it is set up. The Tokkl server itself listens on plain HTTP, and HTTPS comes from a reverse proxy or `tailscale serve` in front of it. Real servers use a public certificate, a certificate from `tailscale cert`, a self-signed certificate, or Tokkl's default of plain HTTP on port 8080.

HTTPS with a publicly trusted certificate or a CA the user installed in Android works (S2). For a self-signed certificate the user reads a disclaimer and trusts it. The app shows the certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, trusts that certificate only, and asks again when it changes. It never accepts any certificate without a check (S3). The app pins the certificate itself, separate from Android's trust store.

Plain `http://` works only after a warning stronger than the self-signed one. The warning says the token travels unencrypted unless the network encrypts it, as Tailscale does (S4). Android 9 and later block cleartext unless the app opts in, and Android 7 and later trust only system CAs by default. So the network security config permits cleartext and trusts user-added CAs.

[credential-related line omitted]

type: map
title: 'Tokkl and Toggl API endpoints, auth and Tokkl-only extensions'
description: >-

Tokkl implements a subset of the Toggl Track API v9 at `https://<server>/api/v9` and of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`. A client that accepts a base URL needs no other change to talk to it. The server URL may carry a path, such as `https://example.com/tokkl`. Toggl Track's API lives at `api.track.toggl.com`.

[credential-related line omitted]

Tokkl adds `role` and `time_resolution_minutes` to `GET /api/v9/me`, and the server snaps stopped entries to that resolution. Tokkl-only endpoints outside the Toggl API cover the trash (`GET/DELETE /api/app/trash`, `DELETE /api/app/trash/{id}`) and undoing a delete (`POST /api/app/time_entries/{id}/restore`). Favorites are a Tokkl key in `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.

The first version targets Tokkl only. Toggl Track comes later by pointing the app at Toggl's base URL, and that version hides the Tokkl-only features.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings.md
---
type: practice
title: 'Use Tokkl''s palette, WCAG AA contrast and English string resources'
description: >-
  Always Tokkl's palette, with wallpaper dynamic colour only as an opt-in. WCAG
  AA contrast, colour never the only signal, text in resources.
tags:
  - ui
  - accessibility
  - i18n
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-tokkl-palette-wcag-english-strings
kk_derived_from:
  - docs/PRD.md
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: medium
---
Components come from Compose Material 3. Colour is always Tokkl's palette, never the wallpaper. Dynamic color is off and exists at most as an opt-in setting (G2). The theme is light, dark, or follows the system (G1).

Anybody, bundled with the app, sets the running clock, totals and headings. Everything else uses the system font.

Contrast meets WCAG AA as in Tokkl: 4.5:1 for text and 3:1 for non-text elements. Colour is never the only signal for a state. Every control has a TalkBack label, and layouts survive the largest font scale. Tokkl's sixth principle applies as written. Any style that costs a readable number, a reachable control or a passing test gets dropped.

The app is English only. All text lives in string resources. No other language is planned, and contributors can add translations.

---
The app has its own version, independent of Tokkl's, with its own `v*` tags and changelog.

semantic-release does not support a first release below 1.0.0. So the first release is a hand-made `v0.1.0` tag, and semantic-release counts on from it. A commit marked as a breaking change moves the app to 1.0.0.

semantic-release runs on pushes to `main` with Tokkl's release rules and `v${version}` tags. It writes `CHANGELOG.md` and the version into the Gradle build, derives `versionCode` from the version, and attaches the signed release APK to the GitHub release.

The app ships on F-Droid only, with no Play Store listing planned. The signed APK on each GitHub release serves anyone who sideloads.

`compileSdk` is 37 because Compose UI 1.12 declares `minCompileSdk` 37 in its AAR metadata, and a lower value fails AGP's AAR metadata check. `targetSdk` stays at 36, the API level of the emulator `tokkl-api36`, so lint reports `OldTargetApi` and the project accepts that warning. `gradle.properties` allows for the other services on this machine. `org.gradle.jvmargs` caps the daemon heap at `-Xmx2g` and metaspace at 768m, and `kotlin.compiler.execution.strategy=in-process` compiles Kotlin inside the Gradle daemon instead of a second long-lived Kotlin daemon.

The wrapper is Gradle 9.8.1. `distributionSha256Sum` in `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties` pins its checksum, so change the sum whenever `distributionUrl` changes.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-gradle-no-daemon-in-parallel-worktrees.md
---
type: practice
title: Build with --no-daemon in parallel worktrees and never run --stop
description: >-
  ./gradlew --stop ends every daemon of that Gradle version for the user,
  including one serving a build in another worktree.
tags:
  - build
  - gradle
  - agents
  - worktrees

---
Gradle daemons are shared by every project of the same user and Gradle version, through the registry in `~/.gradle/daemon/<version>`. `./gradlew --stop` therefore stops all of them, including the daemon of a build running in another git worktree, and that build dies. Never run `--stop` while another build may be running. When more than one worktree builds at the same time, run each build as `./gradlew --no-daemon <task>` so it gets its own single-use process and shares no daemon.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells.md
---
type: map
title: 'JDK and Android SDK on this machine, and how agent shells reach them'
description: >-
  Temurin 21 in ~/.local/share/jdk/21, SDK in ~/Android/Sdk. Agents reach java,
  adb and the SDK tools through wrappers in ~/.local/bin.
tags:
  - android
  - tooling
  - agents
  - build
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to: []
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
On this machine the Temurin JDK 21 lives in `~/.local/share/jdk/21` and the Android SDK in `~/Android/Sdk`. One file, `~/.config/android-env.sh`, sets `JAVA_HOME`, `ANDROID_HOME` and `PATH`. `~/.profile` sources it, and so does the top of `~/.bashrc`, above the check that returns early for non-interactive shells. README.md "Building" lists the versions and install commands.

Claude Code's Bash tool resets `PATH` to the `PATH` of the Claude Code process, so agent shells never get the JDK and SDK directories, even when `JAVA_HOME` and `ANDROID_HOME` are set. That `PATH` does include `~/.local/bin`. There, `java`, `adb`, `sdkmanager`, `avdmanager` and `emulator` are symlinks to `~/.local/libexec/android-env/tool-wrapper`, which sources `android-env.sh` and runs the real tool. Symlinking a tool directly breaks `sdkmanager` and `avdmanager`, because they find the SDK from their own path.

A shell that reads neither file, such as one under `claude -p`, has no `JAVA_HOME` or `ANDROID_HOME`. A Gradle build there needs `ANDROID_HOME` set by hand or `sdk.dir` in `local.properties`, which `.gitignore` already ignores.

PATH .ai/kenkeep/nodes/practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless.md
---
type: practice
title: 'Test on the plain AOSP API 36 emulator, headless, and stop it afterwards'
description: >-
  AVD tokkl-api36 uses the AOSP image, since ATD images drop SystemUI and the
  launcher. Boot it with -no-window -accel on and kill it after.
tags:
  - testing
  - android
  - emulator
  - energy
kk_schema_version: 3
kk_id: practice-emulator-plain-aosp-image-headless
kk_derived_from: []
kk_relates_to:
  - practice-test-real-tokkl-server-speed-regressions
  - map-jdk-android-sdk-and-agent-shells
kk_depends_on: []
kk_confidence: high
---
The emulator AVD is `tokkl-api36`, built on the plain AOSP image `system-images;android-36;default;x86_64` (Android 16). It is not a Google APIs, Play Store or Automated Test Device (ATD) image. ATD images are smaller, but they remove SystemUI and the launcher, and the notification, Quick Settings tile and widget tests need both.

Boot it without a window: `emulator -avd tokkl-api36 -no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot -accel on`. With `-accel on` the emulator refuses to start when KVM is missing, instead of falling back to slow software emulation. `emulator -accel-check` says whether KVM works.

This machine is an always-on homelab server tuned for low idle power. When the tests finish, run `adb emu kill` and then `adb kill-server`, so neither the emulator nor the adb server keeps running. To look for leftovers, use `pgrep -x adb` and `pgrep -af qemu-system`. `pgrep -f adb` also matches `mariadbd` and the shell that runs the command.

---

# Turn off cloud backup and device transfer for all app data

## Problem

PRD S8 keeps every app file out of Android backup and device transfer (docs/PRD.md:97). The only backup rule in the tracker is TKA-0011's, and it excludes the secret store alone (.ai/sift/open/server-connection/hardening/TKA-0011--keystore-secrets.md:33). The Room cache and the change queue would still go to cloud backup and to a new phone. After a restore, the queue could replay old changes. The restored token could not be decrypted either, because Keystore keys do not move to the new phone.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:97
- .ai/sift/open/server-connection/hardening/TKA-0011--keystore-secrets.md:33

## Direction

- Set `android:allowBackup="false"` on `<application>` in the manifest.
- Point `android:dataExtractionRules` at an XML file whose `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>` sections each exclude every domain (`root`, `file`, `database`, `sharedpref`, `external`, each with `path="."`). On Android 12 and later, `allowBackup="false"` alone does not stop device-to-device transfer on some phones, as PRD S8 notes with a link to [Auto Backup](https://developer.android.com/identity/data/autobackup).
- For Android 11 and lower (API 26 to 30), point `android:fullBackupContent` at rules that exclude the same domains.
- On a new phone the user signs in again, as S8 says.
- This makes TKA-0011's own exclusion of the secret store redundant. TKA-0011 will be edited separately to rely on this ticket, so leave it alone here.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The merged manifest sets `android:allowBackup="false"`.
- [ ] The `android:dataExtractionRules` file excludes `root`, `file`, `database`, `sharedpref` and `external` in both `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`.
- [ ] The `android:fullBackupContent` file excludes the same domains, covering API 26 to 30.
- [ ] A test or lint check proves backup is off. Either a unit test parses the merged manifest and both rule files, or `adb shell bmgr backupnow <package>` on the emulator reports the package as not eligible.

# Build the Toggl API v9 and Reports v3 client with Basic auth and path-bearing base URLs

## Problem

[credential-related line omitted]

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:50
- docs/PRD.md:51
- docs/PRD.md:88
- docs/PRD.md:190
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

- Build one OkHttp client and decode JSON with kotlinx.serialization. The app creates exactly one `OkHttpClient` instance, and every request goes through it.
- The client takes a base URL that may include a path. It appends `/api/v9` for the Toggl API and `/reports/api/v3` for reports. A trailing slash on the base URL must not drop the path or produce a double slash.
[credential-related line omitted]
- Map responses to typed errors. 403 is an auth failure. 429 is rate limiting and carries the `Retry-After` seconds. A network failure, such as no route or a timeout, has its own error type, separate from HTTP errors.
- Later tickets extend this client and never build a second one. TKA-0021 adds the trust manager for the pinned self-signed certificate, and TKA-0017 adds the host guard.
- JVM tests cover URL building, auth headers and error mapping. Checks against a real server come in TKA-0016.

## Acceptance criteria

[credential-related line omitted]
- [ ] A 403 maps to a typed auth error, and a 429 maps to a typed rate-limit error that carries the `Retry-After` seconds.
- [ ] A network failure maps to an error type distinct from HTTP errors.
- [ ] The app has exactly one `OkHttpClient` instance, and it decodes responses with kotlinx.serialization.

---

# Lint commit messages in CI because git revert and cherry-pick skip the commit-msg hook

## Problem

The husky commit-msg hook never sees messages that `git revert` writes, so a revert committed that way can carry a ticket ID in the subject or a credit trailer even after TKA-0078. The hook also does not run for commits made with `--no-verify`, in a clone or worktree without `npm install`, or through a web UI. Nothing in the planned CI lints commit messages, and semantic-release reads reverts on `main`.

## Evidence

- Checked on 2026-10-18 with git 2.43.0 and `GIT_TRACE=1`. `git revert -e HEAD~2`, with an editor that wrote `Revert "feat: add probe a TKA-0001"`, ran `prepare-commit-msg` and `post-commit` and never `commit-msg`. The commit was created and `git log -1 --format=%s` showed the ticket ID. A plain `git commit` in the same worktree ran `commit-msg`. The probe branches were deleted afterwards.
- The same message piped into `npx --no -- commitlint` after TKA-0078 exits 1, so the config catches it; only the hook never calls it.
- ../tokkl/.github/workflows/ci.yml:26-37 lints the pushed range with `commitlint --from "$FROM" --to "$TO"` and falls back to `--last` after a force push. TKA-0013 plans this repo's `ci.yml` and does not list that step.
- absent: .github/workflows/ci.yml

## Direction

- Add the commit-message step to the `ci.yml` that TKA-0013 creates, following ../tokkl/.github/workflows/ci.yml:26-37: checkout with `fetch-depth: 0`, setup-node 22.12 or later, `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, then the range lint with the `--last` fallback. Pin every action by SHA as TKA-0013 does.
- This needs TKA-0013's `ci.yml`, which is wave 3. Add `depends_on: [TKA-0013]` and move this ticket to wave 3 or later when scheduling.
- The repo has no remote yet, so the step can be written now but not run. Checking it waits for the move to GitHub.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `ci.yml` runs commitlint over the pull request's commits or the pushed range, with the `--last` fallback after a force push.
- [ ] A pushed `Revert "feat: x"` commit whose subject carries a `TKA-` ID fails the job.
- [ ] Verified after the move to GitHub: the first run on `main` is green.

title: Declare @commitlint/is-ignored in package.json since the config imports it

# Declare @commitlint/is-ignored in package.json since the config imports it

## Problem

TKA-0078 made commitlint.config.js import `@commitlint/is-ignored`, but package.json does not list it. It resolves today only because npm hoists it from `@commitlint/cli` → `@commitlint/lint`. A stricter package manager, a lockfile regeneration that nests it, or a commitlint release that drops it from the CLI's tree would break the hook with a module-not-found error, and the hook would then reject every commit. The coordinator's "no new npm dependencies" constraint for TKA-0078 caused this; declaring a package the config imports directly is not a new dependency in substance.

## Evidence

- commitlint.config.js:2 has `import isIgnored from '@commitlint/is-ignored';`.
- commitlint.config.js:1 comments that it ships with `@commitlint/cli`, which is why package.json does not list it.
- package.json:10 opens devDependencies, which list only `@commitlint/cli`, `@commitlint/config-conventional` and `husky` under devDependencies.
- `npm ls @commitlint/is-ignored` on 2026-10-18 shows `@commitlint/cli@21.2.3 → @commitlint/lint@21.2.3 → @commitlint/is-ignored@21.2.3`.

## Direction

- Add `@commitlint/is-ignored` to devDependencies with the same caret range as the other commitlint packages (`^21.2.3`), regenerate package-lock.json with npm, and drop the comment on commitlint.config.js:1.
- Do not change the config's logic.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] package.json lists `@commitlint/is-ignored` in devDependencies and `npm ls @commitlint/is-ignored` shows it as a direct dependency.
- [ ] `npm ci` exits 0, and the TKA-0078 checks still hold: `Revert "feat: implement TKA-0001"` with a `Co-Authored-By` trailer exits 1, and `feat: add x` with `Refs: TKA-0001` exits 0.

# Stop a "Merge branch" line in a commit body from skipping commitlint's format rules

## Problem

commitlint's default ignore for merge commits matches any line of the message, not just the header. A non-Conventional subject with a body line such as `Merge 

exec
/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
ids=['0069','0009','0013','0082','0083','0084','0012','0010','0006','0011','0005','0014','0090','0089','0007','0008','0085'];s=''
for i in ids:
 for p in Path('/home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android/.ai/sift/archive').rglob('TKA-'+i+'--*.md'):s+='\\nPATH '+str(p)+'\\n'+p.read_text()
print(s[14600:21900])
PY" in /home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android--review-w3
 succeeded in 0ms:
branch y` is therefore treated as a merge and skips the Conventional Commits rules. TKA-0078 keeps the two custom rules (ticket ID in the header, credit trailers) running for such messages, so only the format rules are bypassed. semantic-release ignores a non-Conventional commit, so the risk is a commit that silently never counts toward a release.

## Expected behaviour

Only a message whose header is a merge subject is treated as a merge. `added a thing` with a body line `Merge branch y` fails `type-empty` and `subject-empty` like any other non-Conventional message.

## Steps to reproduce

1. In a checkout where `npm install` has run, pipe `added a thing`, a blank line and `Merge branch y` into `npx --no -- commitlint`.
2. It exits 0 on 2026-10-18 at commit 7359253, and also exited 0 at d3cf48c before TKA-0078.
3. `added a thing` alone exits 1.

## Evidence

- commitlint.config.js:60 defines `ignoreCleanDefaults`, which calls `isIgnored(message)` on the whole message.
- commitlint.config.js:64 sets `defaultIgnores: false` and `ignores: [ignoreCleanDefaults]`.
- Reproduced by the coordinator on 2026-10-18 on both commits listed in Steps to reproduce.

## Direction

- Pass only the header, or only the merge-related checks applied to the header, when deciding that a message is a merge; keep the other default ignores as TKA-0078 left them.
- Keep the TKA-0078 checks and real `git merge --no-ff` and `--log` messages passing.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The message in Steps to reproduce exits nonzero.
- [ ] A `git merge --no-ff` message and a `git merge --log` message still exit 0.
- [ ] The TKA-0078 cases still hold: reverts or fixups with a ticket ID or credit trailer exit 1, and a clean `Revert "feat: add x"` with `Refs: TKA-0001` exits 0.

# Port Tokkl's duration and time-of-day parsing to Kotlin and test it against the TypeScript cases

## Problem

Manual entry needs Tokkl's parsing of `9`, `930`, `9:30`, `4pm`, entries across midnight, and durations like `1h 30m`, `90` and `1,5h` (docs/PRD.md:110). PRD section 6.1 asks for a Kotlin port checked against the same cases as the TypeScript tests (docs/PRD.md:191), and the test plan runs JVM unit tests (docs/PRD.md:215). The duration parser is `parseDuration` (../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:16) with its cases at ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts:6, and TKA-0002 records where the time-of-day parser lives. The app has no source or tests yet, since neither `app/src/main/` nor `app/src/test` exists. Without a Kotlin port tested against the TypeScript cases, the phone reads input differently from the web app, which is PRD risk 5 (docs/PRD.md:254).

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:110
- docs/PRD.md:191
- docs/PRD.md:215
- docs/PRD.md:254
- ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:16
- ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts:6
- absent: app/src/main/
- absent: app/src/test

## Direction

Write pure Kotlin functions under `app/src/main/` that import nothing from `android.*` or `androidx.*`:

- A port of `parseDuration` from ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:16. It returns whole seconds, or null for input it rejects, as the original does.
- A port of the time-of-day parser at the path TKA-0002 records in PRD section 6.1.

Match the TypeScript output wherever Kotlin and JavaScript differ. One trap: the original calls `replace(',', '.')` on a string (../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts:17), which in JavaScript replaces only the first comma, while Kotlin's `String.replace` replaces every one.

JVM tests under `app/src/test` replay every case from the TypeScript test files, starting with ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts:6. Copy the cases verbatim as data and do not edit them. A comment at the top of each test file names the TypeScript source file and the tokkl commit the cases came from, so a later diff against tokkl shows drift (docs/PRD.md:254). The tests run as plain JVM unit tests (docs/PRD.md:215).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every case from ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts passes in a JVM test, including the ones that expect null.
- [ ] Every time-of-day case from the test file TKA-0002 names passes in a JVM test.
- [ ] Each test file records the TypeScript file and the tokkl commit its cases came from.
- [ ] The parser source has no Android dependencies and its tests run without a device or emulator.

# Add a network security config that permits cleartext and trusts user-added CAs

## Problem

Since Android 9 an app cannot use cleartext HTTP unless it opts in, and since Android 7 it trusts only system CAs by default. The Tokkl server listens on plain HTTP, and HTTPS comes from a reverse proxy or `tailscale serve` in front of it. Some servers also use a certificate from a CA the user installed on the phone. Without an opt-in, users of either kind of server cannot connect.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:55
- ../tokkl/packages/api/src/index.ts:20
- docs/PRD.md:89
- docs/PRD.md:91
- docs/PRD.md:197
- absent: app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml

## Direction

- Add `res/xml/network_security_config.xml` with a `base-config` that sets `cleartextTrafficPermitted="true"` and has trust anchors for `system` and `user` certificates.
- Reference it from the manifest with `android:networkSecurityConfig`.
- Keep the self-signed pin out of this file. TKA-0021 pins that certificate in app code, separate from Android's trust store.
- This file only allows cleartext. The warning before a token goes to an `http://` server is TKA-0022.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The manifest references the network security config.
- [ ] The config's `base-config` permits c

# Fail the build when a dependency pulls in Play Services, Firebase or telemetry

## Problem

F-Droid refuses apps with proprietary libraries such as Google Play Services or Firebase. Principle 1 forbids telemetry, analytics, crash reporting and ads, and every dependency must be free software. One transitive artifact would break both rules. Nothing in the build checks for it, and `app/build.gradle.kts` does not exist yet.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:66 forbids telemetry, analytics, crash reporting and ads, and any request to a host other than the configured server.
- docs/PRD.md:193 requires every dependency to be free software.
- docs/PRD.md:223 requires a free toolchain with no proprietary libraries such as Google Play Services or Firebase, per F-Droid's inclusion policy.
- absent: app/build.gradle.kts

## Direction

- Add a Gradle check to the build from TKA-0004 and wire it into `check`.
- The check resolves the release runtime classpath and fails when any module's group is on a deny list. The failure names each offending artifact.
- Keep the deny list in one file, with a comment on each entry saying why it is there.
- The list covers `com.google.android.gms`, `com.google.firebase` and `com.google.android.play`, plus known analytics and crash-reporting groups.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `./gradlew check` fails and names the artifact when a denied group reaches the release classpath.
- [ ] `./gradlew check` passes on the current dependencies.
- [ ] The deny list covers at least `com.google.android.gms`, `com.google.firebase` and `com.google.android.play`.

---

# Derive versionCode from versionName in the Gradle build, starting at 0.1.0

## Problem

F-Droid reads `versionCode` from the Gradle build at each tag. semantic-release will later write the version into the same build. Without one place for the version and one formula for `versionCode`, the two can disagree. The app has its own version starting at 0.1.0, and the build file that would hold it does not exist yet.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:225 says that with `UpdateCheckMode: Tags`, F-Droid checks every tag and reads the version code from the Gradle build.
- docs/PRD.md:235 gives the app its own version, starting at 0.1.0.
- docs/PRD.md:245 has semantic-release write the version into the Gradle build and derive `versionCode` from it.
- absent: app/build.gradle.kts

## Direction

- Keep the version string in exactly one file that semantic-release can rewrite. Set `versionName` from it, starting at 0.1.0.
- Derive `versionCode` with a formula that rises on every semver bump. Write the formula in a comment in the build file.
- F-Droid must be able to read `versionCode` at each tag. Either keep `versionCode` a literal that the release step rewrites, or document the `UpdateCheckData` pattern F-Droid needs.
- Record the 0.1.0 `versionCode` with the resolution. TKA-0064 names a changelog file after it.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The version string lives in one file.
- [ ] `versionName` is 0.1.0 and `versionCode` follows the formula documented in the build file.
- [ ] `./gradlew assembleRelease` produces an APK with `versionName` 0.1.0.
- [ ] This ticket's resolution states how F-Droid reads `versionCode` at a tag.

# Run the denied-dependency check in the CI Gradle job

## Problem

TKA-0005 added `:app:checkDeniedDependencies`, which fails when Play Services, Firebase, an analytics SDK or another denied group reaches the release runtime classpath. Only `check` depends on it. The CI Gradle job runs `assembleDebug lint testDebugUnitTest`, never `check`, so a pull request that adds a denied dependency passes CI. A contributor who never runs `check` locally would only learn about it from F-Droid's reviewers.

## Evidence

- .github/workflows/ci.yml:34 runs `./gradlew assembleDebug lint testDebugUnitTest --stacktrace`.
- app/build.gradle.kts:188 makes `check` depend on `checkDeniedDependencies`; no other task depends on it.
- docs/PRD.md:68 rules out telemetry, analytics, crash reporting and ads, and docs/PRD.md:228 rules out proprietary libraries for F-Droid.

## Direction

- Run the check in the existing `gradle` job, either by switching to `./gradlew assembleDebug check --stacktrace` or by adding `:app:checkDeniedDependencies` to the current task list.
- `check` already runs `lint` and `testDebugUnitTest`, so the job runs nothing twice.
- Keep the job's pinned actions as they are.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Verified after the move to GitHub: a branch that adds a dependency from a denied group fails the job and the log names the module.

# Move LaunchScreenTest to the v2 createAndroidComposeRule

## Problem

Every compile of the instrumented tests warns that `createAndroidComposeRule` in `androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4` is deprecated. The warning names `androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createAndroidComposeRule` as the replacement. The v2 rule runs coroutines on a `StandardTestDispatcher`, which queues tasks instead of running them at once. A test that relies on immediate execution then needs explicit synchronization. Later Compose UI tests will copy this one, so the deprecated rule would spread.

## Evidence

- app/src/androidTest/java/com/github/e0ipso/tokkl/LaunchScreenTest.kt:11 calls the deprecated `createAndroidComposeRule<MainActivity>()`.
- `./gradlew --no-daemon connectedDebugAndroidTest` on 2026-10-18 (Compose BOM 2026.09.00) printed `w: .../LaunchScreenTest.kt:11:23 ... is deprecated. Use androidx.compose.ui.test.junit4.v2.createAndroidComposeRule instead.`

## Direction

- Import the v2 `createAndroidComposeRule` and keep the test's assertions.
- Add explicit synchronization only if the test fails under the v2 dispatcher.
- Run it with `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh` (TKA-0006).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Compiling the instrumented tests prints no deprecation warning for `createAndroidComposeRule`.
- [ ] LaunchScreenTest passes on the emulator through `scripts/instrumented-tests.sh`.

# Build the Tokkl Material 3 theme from the web colour tokens, light and dark

## Problem

No theme exists, so the app cannot show Tokkl's sand, ink and sunset orange in light or its navy and neon in dark. The PRD makes Tokkl's palette the default on Material 3 components, never the wallpaper. Every screen after this one needs the theme's colours, and the running and billable states need theirs. Principle 5 also means a colour that costs readable text gets dropped, so the mapping has to prove its contrast.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:70 sets principle 5. Any style that costs a readable number, a reachable control or a passing test gets dropped.
- docs/PRD.md:74 picks Material 3 through Compose Material 3.
- docs/PRD.md:75 sets the colour direction. Always Tokkl's palette, with the styles.css colour tokens as the source of truth, and dynamic colour off or at most an opt-in.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:161 sets `--bg: light-dark(#f4e7cf, #0b1026)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:164 sets `--text: light-dark(#1c1a2b, #f3f1ff)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:167 sets `--primary: light-dark(#a8380a, #ff8c42)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:169 sets `--secondary: light-dark(#00727c, #2ee6e6)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:170 sets `--accent-pink: light-dark(#d81b72, #ff4fa8)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:171 sets `--accent-lime: light-dark(#4f7a00, #b6ff3b)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:187 sets `--state-running: var(--accent-pink)`.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:189 sets `--state-billable: light-dark(#4a7c00, #b6ff3b)`.
- absent: app/src/main/res/values/themes.xml
- absent: app/src/main/

## Direction

Build Compose Material 3 light and dark colour schemes from the styles.css tokens. In `light-dark()` the first value is the light one. Map them this way:

- background from `--bg`, styles.css:161
- text on background and surface from `--text`, styles.css:164
- primary from `--primary`, styles.css:167
- secondary from `--secondary`, styles.css:169
- the pink and lime accents from `--accent-pink` and `--accent-lime`, styles.css:170 and :171
- the running state from `--state-running`, styles.css:187, which resolves to the pink accent
- the billable state from `--state-billable`, styles.css:189, which differs from the lime accent in light

Keep every value in one Kotlin file, with a comment naming the styles.css line it came from. Roles that no cited token covers, such as surface or onPrimary, take the matching token from the same `:root` block, with the same kind of comment.

Expose the running and billable state colours through the theme, for example as an extra colour set provided next to the Material scheme. Screens use them together with a non-colour cue such as an icon or a label, never alone.

Add an XML window theme in res/values/themes.xml, with a night variant, whose window background matches the scheme background. Launch then shows no white or black flash.

Dynamic colour stays off. The theme takes a dynamic colour flag that defaults to off, and TKA-0015 adds the opt-in setting that sets it.

A JVM test computes WCAG contrast for every pair the theme defines, in both schemes. Text pairs need 4.5:1 and non-text pairs need 3:1. A failing pair fails the build.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The light and dark schemes match the cited styles.css values, and each value's comment names its styles.css line.
- [ ] A JVM test checks 4.5:1 for text pairs and 3:1 for non-text pairs, in light and dark, and passes.
- [ ] No dynamic colour is used unless the setting from TKA-0015 is on.
- [ ] The theme exposes running and billable state colours for use alongside a non-colour cue.
- [ ] Launch shows the theme's background with no flash, in light and dark.

# Bundle the Anybody font with its OFL licence for the clock, totals and headings

## Problem

The running clock, the totals and the headings cannot use Anybody, Tokkl's display face. An F-Droid build that bundles the font must also carry its OFL notice. Tokkl ships Anybody only as a woff2 variable font, and Android's res/font cannot load woff2, so the file cannot be copied over as it is.

## Evidence

- docs/PRD.md:76 sets the type direction. Anybody for the running clock, totals and headings, bundled with the app under the SIL Open Font License 1.1, and the system font for everything else.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/OFL.txt:1 names the font's authors and upstream project, github.com/Etcetera-Type-Co/Anybody, and declares no Reserved Font Name.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/OFL.txt:3 licenses the font under the SIL Open Font License 1.1.
- ../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css:269 opens the `@font-face` rule that loads Anybody from a single woff2 file.
- absent: app/src/main/res/font/

## Direction

Get a TTF of Anybody from the upstream project named in OFL.txt:1, or convert Tokkl's woff2 to TTF. The OFL declares no Reserved Font Name, so a converted file may keep the name Anybody. Bundle it in res/font.

Use it for the theme's display and headline styles, the running clock and the totals. Body text keeps the system font.

Ship OFL.txt unchanged in the APK, and add a licences entry in the app that opens its text. All labels for that entry live in English string resources.

Record the font's source URL, version and any conversion step next to the font file. If the build rejects a text file inside res/font, keep the note beside the bundled OFL.txt and name the font file in it.

Check the clock, totals and headings at the largest font scale and keep them from clipping or overlapping.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The clock, the totals and the headings use Anybody, and other text uses the system font.
- [ ] The OFL text ships in the app and can be opened from it.
- [ ] The font's source and version are recorded next to it.
- [ ] The clock, totals and headings survive the largest font scale without clipping.

# Add an adaptive launcher icon made from Tokkl's artwork

## Problem

The app has no launcher icon, so the launcher and the app list show Android's generic placeholder. No open ticket adds one. TKA-0064 builds the F-Droid `images/icon.png` from the launcher icon, and TKA-0062 adds shortcuts to a long press on it, so both assume an icon exists. Android lint flags the gap on every run.

## Evidence

- app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:4 opens `<application>` with no `android:icon` or `android:roundIcon`. TKA-0004 left it out.
- `./gradlew lint` on the TKA-0004 branch reports `MissingApplicationIcon` at app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:4.
- .ai/sift/open/ci-and-release/release/TKA-0064--fdroid-metadata.md:33 says "Make `images/icon.png` from the launcher icon."
- docs/PRD.md:163 (N6) puts launcher shortcuts on a long press of the app icon.
- ../tokkl/docs/brand/tokkl-icon-v4-1024.png and ../tokkl/docs/brand/icon-maskable-512.png hold the owner's icon artwork. ../tokkl/scripts/icons.ts:5 derives Tokkl's served icons from docs/brand.
- absent: app/src/main/res/mipmap-anydpi/

## Direction

Build an adaptive icon (`<adaptive-icon>` with foreground, background and monochrome layers) from the artwork in ../tokkl/docs/brand. Keep the important part of the foreground inside the 66 dp safe zone, so circle and squircle masks don't clip it. minSdk is 26, so the adaptive icon covers every supported release and no legacy PNG mipmaps are needed. Prefer vector drawables. If the artwork exists only as PNG, export the layers at xxxhdpi and record the source file and the conversion steps next to the drawables.

Set `android:icon` on `<application>`. Use colours from Tokkl's palette, the same one TKA-0007 uses for the theme. Keep the monochrome layer, which Android 13 and later use for themed icons.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `<application>` sets `android:icon` to an adaptive icon with foreground, background and monochrome layers.
- [ ] `./gradlew lint` no longer reports `MissingApplicationIcon`.
- [ ] On the headless emulator from TKA-0001 the launcher shows the icon, unclipped by the circle mask.
- [ ] The source of the artwork and any conversion steps are recorded in the repo.

roid.application)
     alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.compose)
+    alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.serialization)

+
+// The app version lives in gradle/version.properties, which the release step
+// rewrites. versionName is MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH, and
+//
+//     versionCode = major * 1_000_000 + minor * 1_000 + patch
+//
+// with minor and patch below 1000, so every release gets a higher code than
+// the one before it. 0.1.0 gets 1000.
+//
+// The file also stores the computed number, because F-Droid reads it at each
+// v* tag and cannot evaluate a formula. Its metadata for the app needs
+//
+//     UpdateCheckMode: Tags
+//     UpdateCheckData: gradle/version.properties|versionCode=(\d+)|.|versionName=(\S+)
+//
+// The build fails when the stored number breaks the formula.
+val appVersion = Properties().apply {
+    load(
+        providers.fileContents(layout.settingsDirectory.file("gradle/version.properties"))
+            .asText.get().reader(),
+    )
+}
+val appVersionName: String = appVersion.getProperty("versionName")
+    ?: throw GradleException("gradle/version.properties has no versionName.")
+val appVersionCode: Int = run {
+    val parts = Regex("""(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,3})\.(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,2})\.(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,2})""")
+        .matchEntire(appVersionName)
+        ?.destructured?.toList()?.map { it.toLong() }
+        ?: throw GradleException(
+            "gradle/version.properties: versionName $appVersionName is not MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH " +
+                "with MINOR and PATCH below 1000.",
+        )
+    val (major, minor, patch) = parts
+    val code = major * 1_000_000 + minor * 1_000 + patch
+    if (code !in 1..Int.MAX_VALUE) {
+        throw GradleException("versionName $appVersionName gives $code, outside 1..${Int.MAX_VALUE}.")
+    }
+    code.toInt()
+}
+appVersion.getProperty("versionCode").let { stored ->
+    if (stored != appVersionCode.toString()) {
+        throw GradleException(
+            "gradle/version.properties: versionCode is $stored, but versionName " +
+                "$appVersionName needs $appVersionCode.",
+        )
+    }
 }

+// The dependency graph of the release runtime classpath, for the check below.
+val releaseDependencyGraph = objects.property(ResolvedComponentResult::class.java)
+androidComponents {
+    onVariants(selector().withBuildType("release")) { variant ->
+        releaseDependencyGraph.set(
+            variant.runtimeConfiguration.incoming.resolutionResult.rootComponent,
+        )
+    }
+}
+
+// Fails when the release runtime classpath holds a module from a group in
+// gradle/denied-dependency-groups.txt, or from a group below one of them, and
+// names each such module with the path that pulled it in. `check` runs it.
+val checkDeniedDependencies = tasks.register("checkDeniedDependencies") {
+    group = "verification"
+    description = "Fails when a denied dependency group reaches the release runtime classpath."
+    val denyList = layout.settingsDirectory.file("gradle/denied-dependency-groups.txt").asFile
+    val graph = releaseDependencyGraph
+    inputs.file(denyList)
+    doLast {
+        val reasons = linkedMapOf<String, String>()
+        denyList.readLines().forEachIndexed { index, raw ->
+            val line = raw.trim()
+            if (line.isEmpty() || line.startsWith("#")) return@forEachIndexed
+            val group = line.substringBefore('#').trim()
+            val reason = line.substringAfter('#', "").trim()
+            if (group.isEmpty() || reason.isEmpty() || group.any { it.isWhitespace() }) {
+                throw GradleException(
+                    "${denyList.path}:${index + 1}: write each entry as \"<group> # <reason>\".",
+                )
+            }
+            reasons[group] = reason
+        }
+
+        // Breadth first, remembering which component first reached each one.
+        val root = graph.get()
+        val reachedFrom = mutableMapOf<ResolvedComponentResult, ResolvedComponentResult>()
+        val queue = ArrayDeque(listOf(root))
+        val seen = mutableSetOf(root)
+        val offenders = linkedMapOf<String, MutableList<String>>()
+        while (queue.isNotEmpty()) {
+            val component = queue.removeFirst()
+            val id = component.id
+            if (id is ModuleComponentIdentifier) {
+                val denied = reasons.keys.firstOrNull { id.group == it || id.group.startsWith("$it.") }
+                if (denied != null) {
+                    val path = generateSequence(reachedFrom[component]) { reachedFrom[it] }
+                        .toList()
+                        .asReversed()
+                        .joinToString(" > ") { it.id.displayName }
+                    offenders.getOrPut(denied) { mutableListOf() } +=
+                        "    ${id.displayName}, via $path"
+                }
+            }
+            component.dependencies
+                .filterIsInstance<ResolvedDependencyResult>()
+                .filter { !it.isConstraint }
+                .forEach { dependency ->
+                    if (seen.add(dependency.selected)) {
+                        reachedFrom[dependency.selected] = component
+                        queue.addLast(dependency.selected)
+                    }
+                }
+        }
+        if (offenders.isNotEmpty()) {
+            val report = offenders.entries.joinToString("\n") { (denied, modules) ->
+                "  $denied: ${reasons.getValue(denied)}\n" + modules.joinToString("\n")
+            }
+            throw GradleException(
+                "The release runtime classpath holds modules from groups denied in " +
+                    "gradle/denied-dependency-groups.txt:\n$report",
+            )
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+tasks.named("check") {
+    dependsOn(checkDeniedDependencies)
 }

+/**
+ * The token survives a restart of the app process. The steps run in name
+ * order, and the test orchestrator (app/build.gradle.kts) starts a new process
+ * for each, so step 2 reads what step 1 saved in another process. Step 2 fails
+ * if both run in the same process.
+ */
+@FixMethodOrder(MethodSorters.NAME_ASCENDING)
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    /** Names the process that saved the token. Holds no secret. */
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    @Test
+    fun step1SavesTheToken() {
+        store.clear()
+        store.save(TOKEN)
+        savedBy.writeText(currentProcess())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun step2LoadsTheTokenInANewProcess() {
+        try {
+            assertTrue("step 1 did not run before step 2", savedBy.exists())
+            assertNotEquals("step 1 ran in this same process", savedBy.readText(), currentProcess())
+            assertEquals(TOKEN, store.load())
+        } finally {
+            store.clear()
+            savedBy.delete()
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** A PID can be reused, so the process start time goes with it. */
+    private fun currentProcess(): String = "${Process.myPid()}@${Process.getStartElapsedRealtime()}"
+
+    private companion object {
+        const val TOKEN = "restart-test-token"
+    }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import java.io.IOException
+
+/** The outcome of one API call. */
+sealed interface ApiResult<out T> {
+    data class Success<out T>(val value: T) : ApiResult<T>
+
+    data class Failure(val error: ApiError) : ApiResult<Nothing>
+}
+
+/** Why an API call failed. */
+sealed interface ApiError {
+
+    /** The server answered with a status outside 2xx. */
+    sealed interface Http : ApiError {
+        val status: Int
+    }
+
[credential-related line omitted]
+    data object AuthFailed : Http {
+        override val status: Int get() = 403
+    }
+
+    /**
[credential-related line omitted]
+     * Tokkl. [retryAfterSeconds] is null when `Retry-After` is missing or not
+     * a whole number of seconds.
+     */
+    data class RateLimited(val retryAfterSeconds: Long?) : Http {
+        override val status: Int get() = 429
+    }
+
+    /** Any other status outside 2xx. */
+    data class UnexpectedStatus(override val status: Int) : Http
+
+    /**
+     * No HTTP response arrived: no route, DNS failure, refused connection,
+     * TLS failure or timeout. Not an [Http] error.
+     */
+    data class Network(val cause: IOException) : ApiError
+
+    /** A 2xx response whose body is not the expected JSON. */
+    data class InvalidResponse(val cause: IllegalArgumentException) : ApiError
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import okhttp3.HttpUrl
+import okhttp3.HttpUrl.Companion.toHttpUrlOrNull
+
+/**
+ * The configured server's base URL, which may carry a path such as
+ * `https://example.com/tokkl`. Endpoint URLs keep that path, with the Toggl
+ * API v9 under `/api/v9` and the Reports API v3 under `/reports/api/v3`.
+ */
+class ServerUrl private constructor(private val base: HttpUrl) {
+
+    /** A Toggl API v9 endpoint, such as `api("me")` for `GET /api/v9/me`. */
+    fun api(vararg segments: String): HttpUrl = endpoint(API_V9, segments)
+
+    /** A Reports API v3 endpoint, such as `reports("workspace", "1", "search", "time_entries")`. */
+    fun reports(vararg segments: String): HttpUrl = endpoint(REPORTS_V3, segments)
+
+    /**
+     * Each segment is percent-encoded on its own, so a `/` or `?` inside one
+     * cannot change the path or add a query.
+     */
+    private fun endpoint(prefix: List<String>, segments: Array<out String>): HttpUrl =
+        base.newBuilder()
+            .apply { (prefix + segments).forEach(::addPathSegment) }
+            .build()
+
+    override fun toString(): String = base.toString()
+
+    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean = other is ServerUrl && other.base == base
+
+    override fun hashCode(): Int = base.hashCode()
+
+    companion object {
+        private val API_V9 = listOf("api", "v9")
+        private val REPORTS_V3 = listOf("reports", "api", "v3")
+
+        /**
+         * Returns null unless [url] is an absolute `http` or `https` URL with
+         * no query and no fragment. Empty path segments are dropped, so
+         * `https://example.com/tokkl/` and `https://example.com/tokkl` are the
+         * same server.
+         */
+        fun parse(url: String): ServerUrl? {
+            val parsed = url.toHttpUrlOrNull() ?: return null
+            if (parsed.encodedQuery != null || parsed.encodedFragment != null) return null
+            val base = parsed.newBuilder()
+                .encodedPath("/")
+                .apply { parsed.encodedPathSegments.filter { it.isNotEmpty() }.forEach(::addEncodedPathSegment) }
+                .build()
+            return ServerUrl(base)
+        }
+    }
+}

+ */

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import java.io.IOException
+import kotlinx.serialization.DeserializationStrategy
+import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonElement
+import okhttp3.HttpUrl
+import okhttp3.MediaType.Companion.toMediaType
+import okhttp3.OkHttpClient
+import okhttp3.Request
+import okhttp3.RequestBody.Companion.toRequestBody
+import okhttp3.Response
+
+/**
+ * Client for the Toggl Track API v9 and Reports API v3, as served by Tokkl or
+ * by Toggl Track. Build endpoint URLs with [server], then call [get] or
+ * [send]. Calls block the calling thread, so make them off the main thread.
+ */
+class TogglApiClient(
+    val server: ServerUrl,
+    private val credentials: ApiCredentials,
+    private val httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance,
+) {
+
+    fun <T> get(url: HttpUrl, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
+        send("GET", url, body = null, deserializer)
+
+    /**
+     * Sends [body] as JSON, or no body when it is null. OkHttp refuses a null
+     * body for POST, PUT and PATCH.
+     */
+    fun <T> send(
+        method: String,
+        url: HttpUrl,
+        body: JsonElement?,
+        deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>,
+    ): ApiResult<T> {
+        val request = Request.Builder()
+            .url(url)
[credential-related line omitted]
+            .method(method, body?.let { ApiJson.encodeToString(JsonElement.serializer(), it).toRequestBody(JSON) })
+            .build()
+        return try {
+            httpClient.newCall(request).execute().use { it.toResult(deserializer) }
+        } catch (e: IOException) {
+            ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.Network(e))
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun <T> Response.toResult(deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
+        when {
+            isSuccessful -> decode(body.string(), deserializer)
+            code == 403 -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.AuthFailed)
+            code == 429 -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.RateLimited(retryAfterSeconds(header("Retry-After"))))
+            else -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.UnexpectedStatus(code))
+        }
+
+    private fun <T> decode(text: String, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
+        try {
+            ApiResult.Success(ApiJson.decodeFromString(deserializer, text))
+        } catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {
+            // kotlinx.serialization's SerializationException is an IllegalArgumentException.
+            ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.InvalidResponse(e))
+        }
+
+    private companion object {
+        val JSON = "application/json; charset=utf-8".toMediaType()
+
+        /** Tokkl sends whole seconds. An HTTP date or a negative number counts as missing. */
+        fun retryAfterSeconds(value: String?): Long? = value?.trim()?.toLongOrNull()?.takeIf { it >= 0 }
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.credentials
+
+import android.content.Context
+import android.security.keystore.KeyGenParameterSpec
+import android.security.keystore.KeyProperties
+import android.util.AtomicFile
+import java.io.File
+import java.io.FileNotFoundException
+import java.io.IOException
+import java.security.GeneralSecurityException
+import java.security.KeyStore
+import javax.crypto.Cipher
+import javax.crypto.KeyGenerator
+import javax.crypto.SecretKey
+import javax.crypto.spec.GCMParameterSpec
+
+/**
+ * Keeps the API token encrypted with an AES-GCM key in the Android Keystore
+ * (docs/PRD.md S5). The key never leaves the Keystore. The app-private file
+ * holds the IV and the ciphertext, never the plain token.
+ *
+ * The store has no backup rules of its own: the app keeps all of its data out
+ * of backup and device transfer, and another phone could not decrypt the file
+ * anyway.
+ *
+ * Calls do disk and Keystore I/O, so make them off the main thread.
+ */
[credential-related line omitted]
+
+    private val file = AtomicFile(File(context.filesDir, FILE_NAME))
+
+    /** Encrypts [token] and replaces the stored one. */
+    @Throws(GeneralSecurityException::class, IOException::class)
+    fun save(token: String) {
+        synchronized(LOCK) {
+            val cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION)
+            // The Keystore picks a random IV for every encryption.
+            cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, key() ?: createKey())
+            val iv = cipher.iv
+            val ciphertext = cipher.doFinal(token.toByteArray(Charsets.UTF_8))
+            write(byteArrayOf(FORMAT_VERSION, iv.size.toByte()) + iv + ciphertext)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * The stored token, or null when there is none or it no longer decrypts,
+     * for example because the key is gone or the file was changed. Either way
+     * the user has to sign in again.
+     */
+    @Throws(GeneralSecurityException::class, IOException::class)
+    fun load(): String? {
+        synchronized(LOCK) {
+            val bytes = try {
+                file.readFully()
+            } catch (e: FileNotFoundException) {
+                return null
+            }
+            if (bytes.size < HEADER_SIZE || bytes[0] != FORMAT_VERSION) return null
+            val ivEnd = HEADER_SIZE + (bytes[1].toInt() and 0xFF)
+            if (bytes.size < ivEnd + TAG_BITS / 8) return null
+            val key = key() ?: return null
+            return try {
+                val cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION)
+                cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, key, GCMParameterSpec(TAG_BITS, bytes, HEADER_SIZE, ivEnd - HEADER_SIZE))
+                String(cipher.doFinal(bytes, ivEnd, bytes.size - ivEnd), Charsets.UTF_8)
+            } catch (e: GeneralSecurityException) {
+                null
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /** Deletes the ciphertext and the Keystore key. Signing out calls this (TKA-0038). */
+    @Throws(GeneralSecurityException::class)
+    fun clear() {
+        synchronized(LOCK) {
+            file.delete()
+            keyStore().deleteEntry(KEY_ALIAS)
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun write(bytes: ByteArray) {
+        val out = file.startWrite()
+        try {
+            out.write(bytes)
+            file.finishWrite(out)
+        } catch (e: IOException) {
+            file.failWrite(out)
+            throw e
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun key(): SecretKey? = keyStore().getKey(KEY_ALIAS, null) as SecretKey?
+
+    /** Keystore keys cannot be exported: only the Keystore ever sees the key material. */
+    private fun createKey(): SecretKey {
+        val spec = KeyGenParameterSpec.Builder(KEY_ALIAS, KeyProper

exec

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+// Port of parseDuration in ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts at tokkl commit
+// ec84de60087b32e8279c74197bdc94e52164579b. DurationTest replays the cases from duration.test.ts.
+
+private val UNIT_SECONDS = mapOf(
+    "h" to 3600, "hr" to 3600, "hrs" to 3600, "hour" to 3600, "hours" to 3600,
+    "m" to 60, "min" to 60, "mins" to 60, "minute" to 60, "minutes" to 60,
+    "s" to 1, "sec" to 1, "secs" to 1, "second" to 1, "seconds" to 1,
+)
+
+// The TypeScript anchors these with ^ and $; matchEntire does the same here.
+private val COLON = Regex("""(\d+):(\d{1,2})(?::(\d{1,2}))?""")
+private val BARE_INTEGER = Regex("""\d+""")
+private val BARE_DECIMAL = Regex("""\d*\.\d+""")
+private val UNIT = Regex("""(\d+(?:\.\d+)?|\.\d+)$JS_WHITESPACE*([a-z]+)""")
+
+// 2^63, exactly. Every Double at least this large is out of Long's range.
+private const val LONG_LIMIT = 9.223372036854775808E18
+
+/**
+ * Parses what a person types into a duration field and returns whole seconds.
+ *
+ * Accepted: "1:30" (h:mm), "1:30:15" (h:mm:ss), "90" (minutes), "1.5" or "1,5h" (decimal hours
+ * when a unit or separator says so), "1h 30m", "1h30m", "45s". Returns null for anything else.
+ *
+ * As in JavaScript, the arithmetic runs on doubles and only the first comma becomes a dot, which is
+ * what `replace(',', '.')` does there. The TypeScript can return three kinds of number a Long
+ * cannot hold. It returns NaN for a unit named `constructor`, because its unit lookup finds that
+ * key on `Object.prototype`. It returns Infinity once a number of 300-odd digits overflows a
+ * double, and integers from 2^63 up. This port returns null for all three.
+ */
+fun parseDuration(raw: String): Long? {
+    val input = raw.jsTrim().lowercase().replaceFirst(',', '.')
+    if (input == "") return null
+
+    val colon = COLON.matchEntire(input)
+    if (colon != null) {
+        val h = colon.groupValues[1].toDouble()
+        val m = colon.groupValues[2].toDouble()
+        val s = colon.groups[3]?.value?.toDouble() ?: 0.0
+        if (m > 59 || s > 59) return null
+        return wholeSeconds(h * 3600 + m * 60 + s)
+    }
+
+    // A bare integer means minutes, a bare decimal means hours.
+    if (BARE_INTEGER.matches(input)) return wholeSeconds(input.toDouble() * 60)
+

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import kotlin.math.floor
+
+// Helpers that give the ports of Tokkl's TypeScript the JavaScript behaviour where Kotlin's differs.
+
+/**
+ * A regex class of the characters JavaScript's `\s` and `String.prototype.trim` treat as white
+ * space: ECMAScript WhiteSpace and LineTerminator. Java's `\s` matches only ASCII white space, and
+ * Kotlin's `trim()` uses yet another set.
+ */
+internal const val JS_WHITESPACE =
+    """[\t\n\u000B\f\r \u00A0\u1680\u2000-\u200A\u2028\u2029\u202F\u205F\u3000\uFEFF]"""
+
+/** JavaScript's `/\s+/g`. */
+internal val JS_WHITESPACE_RUN = Regex("$JS_WHITESPACE+")
+
+private val JS_TRIM = Regex("""^$JS_WHITESPACE+|$JS_WHITESPACE+\z""")
+
+/** JavaScript's `String.prototype.trim`. */
+internal fun String.jsTrim(): String = replace(JS_TRIM, "")
+
+/** JavaScript's `Math.round`, which rounds halves up. [kotlin.math.round] rounds them to even. */
+internal fun jsRound(x: Double): Double {
+    if (!x.isFinite()) return x
+    val whole = floor(x)
+    return if (x - whole >= 0.5) whole + 1 else whole
+}
