+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.time
+
+import kotlin.math.abs
+
+// Port of parseTimeOfDay in ../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts at tokkl commit
+// 01d4d3475506b65dd7ad99434d4c307c74448373. TimeOfDayTest replays the cases from timeOfDay.test.ts.
+
+// The TypeScript anchors this with ^ and $; matchEntire does the same here.
+private val TIME_OF_DAY = Regex("""(\d{1,2})(?:[:.]?(\d{2}))?(?::\d{2})?(a|am|p|pm)?""")
+
+private const val DAY_MINUTES = 24 * 60
+
+/**
+ * Parses a typed clock time into minutes after midnight.
+ *
+ * Accepted: "9", "930", "0930", "9:30", "9.30", "9:30:15" (seconds dropped), "9a", "9pm", "12am".
+ * Without a suffix, 24-hour input is taken literally. For 12-hour users, AM or PM for 1-11 is
+ * guessed like Toggl does: with [after] (an end time), the first of the two that follows [after],
+ * wrapping past midnight; otherwise whichever is closer to [reference] (a start time, guessed
+ * against now). [reference], [after] and the result are minutes after midnight. An explicit suffix
+ * is never second-guessed.
+ */
+fun parseTimeOfDay(
+    raw: String,
+    twelveHour: Boolean = false,
+    reference: Int? = null,
+    after: Int? = null,
+): Int? {
+    val match = TIME_OF_DAY.matchEntire(raw.jsTrim().lowercase().replace(JS_WHITESPACE_RUN, ""))
+        ?: return null
+    var hour = match.groupValues[1].toInt()
+    val minute = match.groups[2]?.value?.toInt() ?: 0
+    val suffix = match.groups[3]?.value
+    if (minute > 59) return null
+
+    if (suffix != null) {
+        if (hour < 1 || hour > 12) return null
+        hour = if (suffix.startsWith('a')) {
+            if (hour == 12) 0 else hour
+        } else {
+            if (hour == 12) 12 else hour + 12
+        }
+        return hour * 60 + minute
+    }
+    if (hour > 23) return null
+    if (twelveHour && hour >= 1 && hour <= 11) {
+        val am = hour * 60 + minute
+        val pm = am + 12 * 60
+        // Long arithmetic, so any Int reference or after gives JavaScript's answer without overflow.
+        if (after != null) {
+            fun wait(candidate: Int) = (candidate.toLong() - after + DAY_MINUTES) % DAY_MINUTES
+            return if (wait(pm) < wait(am)) pm else am
+        }
+        if (reference != null) {
+            return if (abs(pm.toLong() - reference) < abs(am.toLong() - reference)) pm else am
+        }
+    }
+    return hour * 60 + minute
+}

+/**
+ * Maps the Material 3 roles onto the tokens of one scheme. A role with no token of its own takes
+ * the nearest one, and its comment says which.
+ *
+ * [other] is the opposite scheme, for inversePrimary. [surfaceBright] and [surfaceDim] are the
+ * lightest and darkest surface tokens, which differ between light and dark.
+ */
+private fun colorScheme(t: Tokens, other: Tokens, surfaceBright: Color, surfaceDim: Color) =
+    ColorScheme(
+        primary = t.primary,
+        onPrimary = t.onPrimary,
+        // --state-selected is the web's fill for a selected control, with --text on it.
+        primaryContainer = t.stateSelected,
+        onPrimaryContainer = t.text,
+        // The primary of the opposite scheme, as Material 3 defines it.
+        inversePrimary = other.primary,
+        secondary = t.secondary,
+        // styles.css has no --on-secondary; --on-primary is its only colour for text on a fill.
+        onSecondary = t.onPrimary,
+        secondaryContainer = t.stateSelected,
+        onSecondaryContainer = t.text,
+        // The pink accent. It is also the running state, so it never marks a state alone.
+        tertiary = t.accentPink,
+        onTertiary = t.onPrimary,
+        tertiaryContainer = t.stateSelected,
+        onTertiaryContainer = t.text,
+        background = t.bg,
+        onBackground = t.text,
+        surface = t.surface,
+        onSurface = t.text,
+        surfaceVariant = t.surface2,
+        onSurfaceVariant = t.muted,
+        // Surface itself, so tonal elevation adds no tint and every surface stays a tested colour.
+        surfaceTint = t.surface,
+        inverseSurface = t.text,
+        inverseOnSurface = t.surface,
+        error = t.danger,
+        onError = t.onDanger,
+        errorContainer = t.dangerSoft,
+        onErrorContainer = t.danger,
+        outline = t.border,
+        outlineVariant = t.border,
+        scrim = t.backdrop,

+        surfaceContainerHigh = t.surface,
+        surfaceContainerHighest = t.surface2,
+        surfaceContainerLow = t.surface,
+        surfaceContainerLowest = t.surface,
+        surfaceDim = surfaceDim,
+        // Fixed roles stay the same in light and dark, so both schemes take the light
+        // --state-selected with the light --text on it.
+        primaryFixed = Light.stateSelected,
+        primaryFixedDim = Light.stateSelected,
+        onPrimaryFixed = Light.text,
+        onPrimaryFixedVariant = Light.text,
+        secondaryFixed = Light.stateSelected,
+        secondaryFixedDim = Light.stateSelected,
+        onSecondaryFixed = Light.text,
+        onSecondaryFixedVariant = Light.text,
+        tertiaryFixed = Light.stateSelected,
+        tertiaryFixedDim = Light.stateSelected,
+        onTertiaryFixed = Light.text,
+        onTertiaryFixedVariant = Light.text,
+    )
+
+internal val TokklLightColorScheme =
+    colorScheme(Light, other = Dark, surfaceBright = Light.surface, surfaceDim = Light.surface2)
+
+internal val TokklDarkColorScheme =
+    colorScheme(Dark, other = Light, surfaceBright = Dark.surface2, surfaceDim = Dark.bg)
+
+/**
+ * Tokkl colours with no Material 3 role. Screens show [running] and [billable] together with an
+ * icon or a label, never as the only sign of the state.
+ */
+@Immutable
+class TokklExtraColors(
+    /** A running time entry. Readable as text on surface, and as a mark on any surface. */
+    val running: Color,
+    /** A billable time entry. A mark only, never text. */
+    val billable: Color,
+    /** The lime accent. A mark only, never text. */
+    val accentLime: Color,
+)
+
+internal val TokklLightExtraColors = TokklExtraColors(
+    running = Light.stateRunning,
+    billable = Light.stateBillable,
+    accentLime = Light.accentLime,
+)
+
+internal val TokklDarkExtraColors = TokklExtraColors(
+    running = Dark.stateRunning,
+    billable = Dark.stateBillable,
+    accentLime = Dark.accentLime,
+)

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme
+
+import android.os.Build
+import androidx.compose.foundation.isSystemInDarkTheme
+import androidx.compose.material3.MaterialTheme
+import androidx.compose.material3.dynamicDarkColorScheme
+import androidx.compose.material3.dynamicLightColorScheme
+import androidx.compose.runtime.Composable
+import androidx.compose.runtime.CompositionLocalProvider
+import androidx.compose.runtime.ReadOnlyComposable
+import androidx.compose.runtime.staticCompositionLocalOf
+import androidx.compose.ui.platform.LocalContext
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.TextStyle
+
+private val LocalTokklExtraColors = staticCompositionLocalOf { TokklLightExtraColors }
+
+/**
+ * Material 3 with Tokkl's palette, light or dark.
+ *
+ * [dynamicColor] takes the Material roles from the wallpaper instead, on Android 12 and later.
+ * It stays off unless the user turns on the opt-in setting (docs/PRD.md:75). The running and
+ * billable colours stay Tokkl's either way.
+ */
+@Composable
+fun TokklTheme(
+    darkTheme: Boolean = isSystemInDarkTheme(),
+    dynamicColor: Boolean = false,
+    content: @Composable () -> Unit,
+) {
+    val colorScheme = when {
+        dynamicColor && Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.S -> {
+            val context = LocalContext.current
+            if (darkTheme) dynamicDarkColorScheme(context) else dynamicLightColorScheme(context)
+        }
+        darkTheme -> TokklDarkColorScheme
+        else -> TokklLightColorSchem

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme
+
+import androidx.compose.material3.Typography
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.TextStyle
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.font.Font
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.font.FontFamily
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.font.FontVariation
+import androidx.compose.ui.text.font.FontWeight
+import androidx.compose.ui.unit.sp
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.R
+
+/**
+ * Anybody, Tokkl's display face (docs/PRD.md:76), from the variable font in res/font.
+ * res/raw/anybody_font_source.txt records where the file comes from.
+ *
+ * Tokkl's web app sets it at 85% width and weight 800 (styles.css:306 and :308). The variable
+ * font's default instance is Thin at 100% width, so each weight a style uses needs a Font entry
+ * that sets both axes.
+ */
+private val Anybody = FontFamily(
+    Font(
+        R.font.anybody_wdth_wght,
+        weight = FontWeight.ExtraBold,
+        variationSettings = FontVariation.Settings(FontVariation.weight(800), FontVariation.width(85f)),
+    ),
+)
+
+private val Default = Typography()
+
+private fun TextStyle.inAnybody() = copy(fontFamily = Anybody, fontWeight = FontWeight.ExtraBold)
+
+/** Material 3's type scale with Anybody for display and headline styles; the rest is the system font. */
+internal val TokklTypography = Typography(
+    displayLarge = Default.displayLarge.inAnybody(),
+    displayMedium = Default.displayMedium.inAnybody(),
+    displaySmall = Default.displaySmall.inAnybody(),
+    headlineLarge = Default.headlineLarge.inAnybody(),
+    headlineMedium = Default.headlineMedium.inAnybody(),
+    headlineSmall = Default.headlineSmall.inAnybody(),
+)
+
+/**
+ * The running clock, 2.4rem in the web app (styles.css:562). Tabular digits keep it from
+ * shifting sideways as it counts. The line height stays the font's own, so no scale clips it.
+ */
+internal val ClockTextStyle = TextStyle(
+    fontFamily = Anybody,
+    fontWeight = FontWeight.ExtraBold,
+    fontSize = 36.sp,
+    fontFeatureSettings = "tnum",
+)
+
+/** Day and report totals, 1.5rem in the web app (styles.css:678), with tabular digits. */
+internal val TotalTextStyle = TextStyle(
+    fontFamily = Anybody,
+    fontWeight = FontWeight.ExtraBold,
+    fontSize = 22.sp,
+    fontFeatureSettings = "tnum",
+)

+<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
+<!--
+    The launcher icon. minSdk is 26, so this adaptive icon covers every supported release and
+    the app needs no legacy PNG mipmaps.
+
+    Source: the owner's Tokkl artwork, ../tokkl/docs/brand/tokkl-icon-v4-1024.png
+    (github.com/e0ipso/tokkl at 36bcba9, 1024x1024 RGBA, SHA-256
+    874f7ec9ccb91c4c60b426a4aa75827db1ed96833cdfbcd3b7e24edb3efe3aae). It exists only as PNG,
+    so the layers are PNGs at xxxhdpi: 432x432 px for the 108 dp layer, in drawable-xxxhdpi.
+
+    The stopwatch's smallest enclosing circle in the source, counting pixels with alpha 8 or
+    more, has its centre at (509, 503.5) and a radius of 457.5 px. Scaling the source to
+    286 px and placing it at +74+75 puts that circle at the layer's centre with a radius of
+    32 dp, inside the 66 dp safe zone, so circle and squircle masks never clip it.
+
+    Built with ImageMagick 7.1.1-43 (SRC is the source PNG):
+
+    ic_launcher_foreground.png, the artwork with its alpha below 3.1% cleared:
+      magick SRC ( +clone -alpha extract -black-threshold 3.1% ) -compose CopyOpacity
+        -composite fg.png
+      magick -size 432x432 xc:none ( fg.png -filter Lanczos -resize 286x286 ) -compose over
+        -geometry +74+75 -composite -strip -define png:exclude-chunks=date,time
+        png32:ic_launcher_foreground.png
+
+    ic_launcher_monochrome.png, for themed icons on Android 13 and later: the artwork's dark
+    outline and hands (grey below 20% on opaque pixels), with specks removed:
+      magick SRC ( +clone -alpha off -colorspace gray -threshold 20% -negate )
+        ( -clone 0 -alpha extract -threshold 50% ) -delete 0 -compose multiply -composite
+        -morphology Open Disk:5 -morphology Close Disk:10 mask.png
+      magick -size 432x432 xc:none ( -size 1024x1024 xc:white mask.png -alpha off
+        -compose CopyOpacity -composite -filter Lanczos -resize 286x286 ) -compose over
+        -geometry +74+75 -composite -strip -define png:exclude-chunks=date,time
+        png32:ic_launcher_monochrome.png
+
+    The background layer is the palette colour @color/ic_launcher_background.
+-->
+<adaptive-icon xmlns:android="http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android">

+Where res/font/anybody_wdth_wght.ttf comes from
+
+Font: Anybody by Etcetera Type Co., the variable font with a width axis
+(50 to 150) and a weight axis (100 to 900).
+Project: https://github.com/Etcetera-Type-Co/Anybody
+File: fonts/variable/Anybody[wdth,wght].ttf at commit
+fe7b55cf9d1563348ad95ac8e05f43b81a420c31 (master, 2023-03-10)
+URL: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Etcetera-Type-Co/Anybody/fe7b55cf9d1563348ad95ac8e05f43b81a420c31/fonts/variable/Anybody%5Bwdth,wght%5D.ttf
+Version: 1.114 (name ID 5 reads "Version 1.114;gftools[0.9.25]")
+SHA-256: b184bd7e6ca8348bbaecec98951565729d7e89b7872d4898a1f9981342b5b64c
+Downloaded: 2026-10-18
+
+Conversion: none. The bytes are the upstream TTF's. Only the name changed,
+because Android resource names allow only a-z, 0-9 and _. Tokkl's web app
+serves the woff2 from the same commit
+(../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/anybody-wdth-wght.woff2, the same git
+blob, a3493241a0c23b84369850724137d3067c03b401).
+
+Licence: SIL Open Font License 1.1, with no Reserved Font Name. The text is
+anybody_ofl.txt beside this note, OFL.txt from the same commit, unchanged
+(SHA-256 7f0313b042b462fcae1934436cc747f9fd4433e3b08fd6459a4a5104b0bbd5db).
+It is the same file as ../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/OFL.txt.
+
+This note lives in res/raw because the build accepts only font and XML
+files in res/font.

+    that attribute is ever turned on again (docs/PRD.md S8).
+-->
+<full-backup-content>
+    <exclude domain="root" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="file" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="database" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="sharedpref" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="external" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="device_root" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="device_file" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="device_database" path="." />
+    <exclude domain="device_sharedpref" path="." />
+</full-backup-content>

+        <exclude domain="file" path="." />

diff --git a/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml b/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..4a2f0e4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/app/src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml
@@ -0,0 +1,21 @@
+<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
+<!--
+    Lets the app reach any Tokkl server (docs/PRD.md S2 and S4). Tokkl itself
+    listens on plain HTTP, so cleartext must be allowed; Android 9 and later
+    block it unless the app opts in. Android 7 and later trust only system CAs
+    unless the app also trusts the CAs the user installed.
+
+    A self-signed certificate is pinned in app code (TKA-0021), never here, and
+    the warning before a token goes to an http:// server lives in the sign-in
+    flow (TKA-0022).
+-->
+<network-security-config xmlns:tools="http://schemas.android.com/tools">
+    <base-config
+        cleartextTrafficPermitted="true"
+        tools:ignore="InsecureBaseConfiguration,AcceptsUserCertificates">
+        <trust-anchors>
+            <certificates src="system" />
+            <certificates src="user" />
+        </trust-anchors>
+    </base-config>
+</network-security-config>

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MergedManifest.application
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MergedManifest.xmlResource
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Test
+import org.w3c.dom.Element
+
+/**
+ * No app data may leave the phone through Android backup or device transfer
+ * (docs/PRD.md S8). Reads the manifest that the Android Gradle plugin merged
+ * for this variant and the backup rule files it points at.
+ */
+class BackupRulesTest {
+
+    @Test
+    fun mergedManifestTurnsBackupOff() {
+        assertEquals("false", application().androidAttribute("allowBackup"))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun dataExtractionRulesExcludeEveryDomainFromEveryTransferMode() {
+        val rules = xmlResource(application().androidAttribute("dataExtractionRules"))
+        assertEquals("data-extraction-rules", rules.tagName)
+        for (mode in listOf("cloud-backup", "device-transfer", "cross-platform-transfer")) {
+            val sections = rules.childElements().filter { it.tagName == mode }
+            assertEquals("<$mode> sections", 1, sections.size)
+            assertExcludesEveryDomain("<$mode>", sections.single())
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun fullBackupContentExcludesEveryDomainForApi26To30() {
+        val rules = xmlResource(application().androidAttribute("fullBackupContent"))
+        assertEquals("full-backup-content", rules.tagName)
+        assertExcludesEveryDomain("<full-backup-content>", rules)
+    }
+
+    private fun assertExcludesEveryDomain(where: String, section: Element) {
+        val children = section.childElements()
+        assertTrue("$where has an <include>", children.none { it.tagName == "include" })
+        val excluded = children
+            .filter { it.tagName == "exclude" && it.getAttribute("path") == "." }
+            .map { it.getAttribute("domain") }
+            .toSet()
+        assertEquals("$where whole-domain excludes", ALL_DOMAINS, excluded)
+    }
+
+    private companion object {
+        val ALL_DOMAINS = setOf(
+            "root", "file", "database", "sharedpref", "external",
+            "device_root", "device_file", "device_database", "device_sharedpref",
+        )
+    }
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import java.io.File
+import java.util.Properties
+import javax.xml.parsers.DocumentBuilderFactory
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.w3c.dom.Element
+
+/**
+ * Reads the manifest that the Android Gradle plugin merged for the variant
+ * under test, and the XML resources it points at. Needs
+ * `unitTests.isIncludeAndroidResources`.
+ */
+internal object MergedManifest {
+
+    private const val TEST_CONFIG = "com/android/tools/test_config.properties"
+
+    /** The merged manifest's `<application>` element. */
+    fun application(): Element {
+        val path = testConfig().getProperty("android_merged_manifest")
+            ?: error("test_config.properties has no android_merged_manifest")
+        val manifest = parseXml(File(path))
+        return manifest.childElements().single { it.tagName == "application" }
+    }
+
+    /** Resolves `@xml/name` to the source file. The app has a single resource directory. */
+    fun xmlResource(reference: String): Element {
+        assertTrue("$reference is not an @xml/ reference", reference.startsWith("@xml/"))
+        return parseXml(File("src/main/res/xml/${reference.removePrefix("@xml/")}.xml"))
+    }
+
+    private fun testConfig(): Properties {
+        val stream = javaClass.classLoader!!.getResourceAsStream(TEST_CONFIG)
+            ?: error("$TEST_CONFIG is missing; unitTests.isIncludeAndroidResources must be on")
+        return stream.use { Properties().apply { load(it) } }
+    }
+
+    private fun parseXml(file: File): Element = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance()
+        .apply { isNamespaceAware = true }
+        .newDocumentBuilder()
+        .parse(file)
+        .documentElement
+}
+
+private const val ANDROID_NS = "http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android"
+
+internal fun Element.androidAttribute(name: String): String {
+    assertTrue("<$tagName> has no android:$name", hasAttributeNS(ANDROID_NS, name))
+    return getAttributeNS(ANDROID_NS, name)
+}
+
+internal fun Element.childElements(): List<Element> {
+    val nodes = childNodes
+    return (0 until nodes.length).map { nodes.item(it) }.filterIsInstance<Element>()
+}
+
+/** This element and every element below it, in document order. */
+internal fun Element.selfAndDescendants(): List<Element> =
+    listOf(this) + childElements().flatMap { it.selfAndDescendants() }

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl
+
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MergedManifest.application
+import com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.MergedManifest.xmlResource
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Test
+import org.w3c.dom.Element
+
+/**
+ * The app reaches Tokkl over plain HTTP and over HTTPS with a CA the user
+ * installed (docs/PRD.md S2 and S4). A self-signed certificate is pinned in
+ * app code (TKA-0021), so the config itself holds no pin or certificate.
+ */
+class NetworkSecurityConfigTest {
+
+    @Test
+    fun manifestReferencesTheNetworkSecurityConfig() {
+        assertEquals("@xml/network_security_config", application().androidAttribute("networkSecurityConfig"))
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun baseConfigPermitsCleartextAndTrustsSystemAndUserCas() {
+        val baseConfigs = config().childElements().filter { it.tagName == "base-config" }
+        assertEquals("<base-config> sections", 1, baseConfigs.size)
+        val baseConfig = baseConfigs.single()
+        assertEquals("true", baseConfig.getAttribute("cleartextTrafficPermitted"))
+
+        val anchors = baseConfig.childElements().single { it.tagName == "trust-anchors" }
+        val sources = anchors.childElements()
+            .filter { it.tagName == "certificates" }
+            .map { it.getAttribute("src") }
+        assertEquals(listOf("system", "user"), sources.sorted())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun configHoldsNoPinOrCertificate() {
+        val elements = config().selfAndDescendants()
+        assertTrue("the config has a <pin-set>", elements.none { it.tagName == "pin-set" || it.tagName == "pin" })
+        val certificateSources = elements.filter { it.tagName == "certificates" }.map { it.getAttribute("src") }
+        assertTrue(
+            "the config trusts a certificate resource: $certificateSources",
+            certificateSources.all { it == "system" || it == "user" },
+        )
+    }
+
+    private fun config(): Element {
+        val config = xmlResource(application().androidAt

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api
+
+import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
+import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
+import org.junit.Test
+
+class ServerUrlTest {
+
+    @Test
+    fun hostRootWithAndWithoutTrailingSlash() {
+        for (base in listOf("https://example.com", "https://example.com/")) {
+            val server = parse(base)
+            assertEquals(base, "https://example.com/api/v9/me", server.api("me").toString())
+            assertEquals(
+                base,
+                "https://example.com/reports/api/v3/workspace/1/search/time_entries",
+                server.reports("workspace", "1", "search", "time_entries").toString(),
+            )
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun basePathWithAndWithoutTrailingSlash() {
+        for (base in listOf("https://example.com/tokkl", "https://example.com/tokkl/", "https://example.com/tokkl//")) {
+            val server = parse(base)
+            assertEquals(base, "https://example.com/tokkl/api/v9/me", server.api("me").toString())
+            assertEquals(
+                base,
+                "https://example.com/tokkl/reports/api/v3/workspace/1/search/time_entries",
+                server.reports("workspace", "1", "search", "time_entries").toString(),
+            )
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun deeperPathPortAndPlainHttp() {
+        val server = parse("http://100.64.0.1:8080/apps/tokkl/")
+        assertEquals(
+            "http://100.64.0.1:8080/apps/tokkl/api/v9/workspaces/7/time_entries",
+            server.api("workspaces", "7", "time_entries").toString(),
+        )
+        assertEquals(
+            "http://100.64.0.1:8080/apps/tokkl/reports/api/v3/workspace/7/summary/time_entries",
+            server.reports("workspace", "7", "summary", "time_entries").toString(),
+        )
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun emptySegmentsInsideTheBasePathAreDropped() {
+        assertEquals("https://example.com/a/b/api/v9/me", parse("https://example.com//a//b/").api("me").toString())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun encodedBasePathIsKept() {
+        assertEquals(
+            "https://example.com/my%20tokkl/api/v9/me",
+            parse("https://example.com/my%20tokkl/").api("me").toString(),
+        )
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun endpointSegmentsCannotAddPathOrQuery() {
+        val url = parse("https://example.com/tokkl").api("me/../../admin?x=1")
+        assertEquals("https://example.com/tokkl/api/v9/me%2F..%2F..%2Fadmin%3Fx=1", url.toString())
+        assertNull(url.query)
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun noEndpointSegmentsGivesTheApiRoot() {
+        assertEquals("https://example.com/tokkl/api/v9", parse("https://example.com/tokkl/").api().toString())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun sameServerWithOrWithoutTrailingSlash() {
+        assertEquals(parse("https://example.com/tokkl"), parse("https://example.com/tokkl/"))
+        assertEquals("https://example.com/tokkl", parse("https://example.com/tokkl/").toString())
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun rejectsWhatIsNotAnHttpBaseUrl() {
+        val bad = listOf(
+            "", "example.com", "ftp://example.com", "https://",
+            "https://example.com/tokkl?x=1", "https://example.com/#top",
+        )
+        for (url in bad) {
+            assertNull(url, ServerUrl.parse(url))
+        }
+    }
+
+    private fun parse(url: String): ServerUrl = ServerUrl.parse(url) ?: error("$url did not parse")
+}

+    @Test
+    fun refusedConnectionIsANetworkError() {
+        val url = serverUrl()

+        val error = failure(TogglApiClient(url, credentials).get(url.api("me"), Me.serializer()))
+
+        assertTrue(error.toString(), error is ApiError.Network)
+        assertFalse(error is ApiError.Http)

+    @Test

+        server.enqueue(MockResponse.Builder().onRequestStart(SocketEffect.CloseSocket()).build())

+        val error = failure(client.get(client.server.api("me"), Me.serializer()))
+
+        assertTrue(error.toString(), error is ApiError.Network)
+    }

+    @Test
+    fun timeoutIsANetworkError() {
+        server.enqueue(MockResponse.Builder().onResponseStart(SocketEffect.Stall).build())
+        // Same settings as the shared client, with a read timeout short enough for a test.
+        val impatient = SharedHttpClient.instance.newBuilder().readTimeout(200, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS).build()
+        val url = serverUrl()
+
+        val error = failure(TogglApiClient(url, credentials, impatient).get(url.api("me"), Me.serializer()))
+
+        assertTrue(error.toString(), error is ApiError.Network && error.cause is SocketTimeoutException)
+    }

+    private fun json(code: Int, body: String) = MockResponse.Builder()

+        .body(body)
+        .build()
+
+    private fun failure(result: ApiResult<*>): ApiError =
+        (result as? ApiResult.Failure)?.error ?: error("expected a failure, got $result")

+    /** The TypeScript `options` argument. */
+    private data class Options(
+        val twelveHour: Boolean? = null,
+        val reference: Int? = null,
+        val after: Int? = null,
+    )
+
+    private class Case(val raw: String, val options: Options, val expected: Int?)
+
+    private fun case(raw: String, expected: Int?) = Case(raw, Options(), expected)
+
+    private fun case(raw: String, options: Options, expected: Int?) = Case(raw, options, expected)
+
+    private fun replay(vararg cases: Case) {
+        for (c in cases) {
+            val actual = parseTimeOfDay(
+                c.raw,
+                twelveHour = c.options.twelveHour ?: false,
+                reference = c.options.reference,
+                after = c.options.after,
+            )
+            assertEquals("input \"${c.raw}\" with ${c.options}", c.expected, actual)
+        }
+    }
+
+    @Test
+    fun `parseTimeOfDay handles 24-hour input`() = replay(
+        case("9", 540),
+        case("09:30", 570),
+

+    fun `parseTimeOfDay handles suffixes and 12-hour guessing`() = replay(
+        case("8a", 480),
+        case("4pm", 960),
+        case("12am", 0),
+        case("12pm", 720),
+        case("13pm", null),
+        // At 15:00, "11" means 11 AM and "4" means 4 PM.
+        case("11", Options(twelveHour = true, reference = 900), 660),
+        case("4", Options(twelveHour = true, reference = 900), 960),
+    )
+
+    @Test
+    fun `an explicit suffix on an end time is never guessed`() = replay(
+        // 10am to 9am: the end stays 9 AM, before the start, so it lands on the next day (23 hours).
+        // The TypeScript stores this first result as `start` and then asserts it equals 600.
+        case("10am", Options(twelveHour = true, reference = 600), 600),
+        case("9am", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 600), 540),
+        case("9pm", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 1320), 1260),
+        case("12am", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 600), 0),
+        case("12pm", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 780), 720),
+    )
+
+    @Test
+    fun `an unsuffixed 12-hour end is the first occurrence after the start`() = replay(
+        // 11pm to "1" is 1 AM, two hours later across midnight.
+        case("1", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 23 * 60), 60),
+        // 10am to "9" is 9 PM, not 9 AM the next day.
+        case("9", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 600), 21 * 60),
+        // 8pm to "9:30" is 9:30 PM; 1pm to "11" is 11 PM; 9pm to "8" is 8 AM the next day.
+        case("9:30", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 20 * 60), 21 * 60 + 30),
+        case("11", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 13 * 60), 23 * 60),
+        case("8", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 21 * 60), 8 * 60),
+        // 9am to "10" is 10 AM; "12" and 0/13-23 are not ambiguous.
+        case("10", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 540), 600),
+        case("12", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 600), 720),
+        case("15", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 1000), 900),
+        case("0:30", Options(twelveHour = true, after = 1000), 30),
+    )
+
+    @Test
+    fun `24-hour input ignores the end-time guess`() = replay(
+        case("1", Options(after = 23 * 60), 60),
+        case("9", Options(after = 600), 540),
+        case("9", Options(twelveHour = false, after = 600), 540),
+        case("21:00", Options(after = 600), 1260),
+    )
+}

+package com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.ui.theme
+
+import androidx.compose.material3.ColorScheme
+import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.Color
+import androidx.compose.ui.graphics.toArgb
+import kotlin.math.max
+import kotlin.math.min

+import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
+import org.junit.Test

+/**
+ * WCAG 2 contrast for every pair the Tokkl theme defines, in light and dark: 4.5:1 for text and
+ * 3:1 for other UI parts such as borders and state marks (docs/PRD.md:70 and :75).
+ */
+class ThemeContrastTest {
+
+    @Test
+    fun lightSchemePairsMeetWcagAa() =
+        assertPairsPass("light", TokklLightColorScheme, TokklLightExtraColors)
+
+    @Test
+    fun darkSchemePairsMeetWcagAa() =
+        assertPairsPass("dark", TokklDarkColorScheme, TokklDarkExtraColors)
+
+    @Test
+    fun contrastMatchesWcagReferenceValues() {
+        assertRatio(21.0, Color.Black, Color.White)
+        assertRatio(1.0, Color.White, Color.White)
+        // styles.css:10 records "text on bg" in light as 13.94, rounded down.
+        assertRatio(13.94, Color(0xFF1C1A2B), Color(0xFFF4E7CF))
+    }
+
+    private enum class Kind(val minimum: Double) { TEXT(4.5), NON_TEXT(3.0) }
+
+    private class Pair(val name: String, val foreground: Color, val background: Color, val kind: Kind)
+
+    private fun pairs(s: ColorScheme, x: TokklExtraColors): List<Pair> {
+        val surfaces = listOf(
+            "background" to s.background,
+            "surface" to s.surface,
+            "surfaceVariant" to s.surfaceVariant,
+            "surfaceBright" to s.surfaceBright,
+            "surfaceDim" to s.surfaceDim,
+            "surfaceContainerLowest" to s.surfaceContainerLowest,
+            "surfaceContainerLow" to s.surfaceContainerLow,
+            "surfaceContainer" to s.surfaceContainer,
+            "surfaceContainerHigh" to s.surfaceContainerHigh,
+            "surfaceContainerHighest" to s.surfaceContainerHighest,
+        )
+        val onEverySurface = listOf(
+            Triple("onSurface", s.onSurface, Kind.TEXT),
+            Triple("onSurfaceVariant", s.onSurfaceVariant, Kind.TEXT),
+            Triple("primary", s.primary, Kind.TEXT),
+            Triple("error", s.error, Kind.TEXT),
+            Triple("secondary", s.secondary, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("tertiary", s.tertiary, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("outline", s.outline, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("outlineVariant", s.outlineVariant, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("running", x.running, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("billable", x.billable, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+            Triple("accentLime", x.accentLime, Kind.NON_TEXT),
+        )
+        val surfacePairs = surfaces.flatMap { (surfaceName, surface) ->
+            onEverySurface.map { (name, colour, kind) -> Pair("$name on $surfaceName", colour, surface, kind) }
+        }
+        val fills = listOf(
+            Pair("onBackground on background", s.onBackground, s.background, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("secondary on background", s.secondary, s.background, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("secondary on surface", s.secondary, s.surface, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("running on surface", x.running, s.surface, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onPrimary on primary", s.onPrimary, s.primary, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onSecondary on secondary", s.onSecondary, s.secondary, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onTertiary on tertiary", s.onTertiary, s.tertiary, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onError on error", s.onError, s.error, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onPrimaryContainer on primaryContainer", s.onPrimaryContainer, s.primaryContainer, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onSecondaryContainer on secondaryContainer", s.onSecondaryContainer, s.secondaryContainer, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onTertiaryContainer on tertiaryContainer", s.onTertiaryContainer, s.tertiaryContainer, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onErrorContainer on errorContainer", s.onErrorContainer, s.errorContainer, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("inverseOnSurface on inverseSurface", s.inverseOnSurface, s.inverseSurface, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("inversePrimary on inverseSurface", s.inversePrimary, s.inverseSurface, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onPrimaryFixed on primaryFixed", s.onPrimaryFixed, s.primaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onPrimaryFixed on primaryFixedDim", s.onPrimar

import subprocess
s=subprocess.check_output(['git','diff','aa18ff1f127fd5a2c359c82a8f4abb50b692c639','HEAD']);print(s[116800:124100].decode())
PY" in /home/e0ipso/workspace/apps/tokkl-android--review-w3
 succeeded in 0ms:
yFixed, s.primaryFixedDim, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onPrimaryFixedVariant on primaryFixed", s.onPrimaryFixedVariant, s.primaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onSecondaryFixed on secondaryFixed", s.onSecondaryFixed, s.secondaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onSecondaryFixed on secondaryFixedDim", s.onSecondaryFixed, s.secondaryFixedDim, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onSecondaryFixedVariant on secondaryFixed", s.onSecondaryFixedVariant, s.secondaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onTertiaryFixed on tertiaryFixed", s.onTertiaryFixed, s.tertiaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onTertiaryFixed on tertiaryFixedDim", s.onTertiaryFixed, s.tertiaryFixedDim, Kind.TEXT),
+            Pair("onTertiaryFixedVariant on tertiaryFixed", s.onTertiaryFixedVariant, s.tertiaryFixed, Kind.TEXT),
+        )
+        return surfacePairs + fills
+    }
+
+    private fun assertPairsPass(scheme: String, colours: ColorScheme, extra: TokklExtraColors) {
+        val failures = pairs(colours, extra).mapNotNull { pair ->
+            val ratio = contrast(pair.foreground, pair.background)
+            if (ratio >= pair.kind.minimum) {
+                null
+            } else {
+                "%s: %.2f:1, needs %.1f:1 for %s".format(pair.name, ratio, pair.kind.minimum, pair.kind)
+            }
+        }
+        assertTrue("$scheme scheme pairs below WCAG AA:\n" + failures.joinToString("\n"), failures.isEmpty())
+    }
+
+    private fun assertRatio(expected: Double, a: Color, b: Color) {
+        val ratio = contrast(a, b)
+        assertTrue("expected $expected, got $ratio", ratio >= expected && ratio < expected + 0.01)
+    }
+
+    /** The WCAG 2 contrast ratio, from the relative luminance of each sRGB colour. */
+    private fun contrast(a: Color, b: Color): Double {
+        val la = luminance(a)
+        val lb = luminance(b)
+        return (max(la, lb) + 0.05) / (min(la, lb) + 0.05)
+    }
+
+    private fun luminance(colour: Color): Double {
+        val argb = colour.toArgb()
+        fun channel(shift: Int): Double {
+            val c = ((argb shr shift) and 0xFF) / 255.0
+            return if (c <= 0.04045) c / 12.92 else ((c + 0.055) / 1.055).pow(2.4)
+        }
+        return 0.2126 * channel(16) + 0.7152 * channel(8) + 0.0722 * channel(0)
+    }
+}

+# Dependency groups the app must never ship. `./gradlew check` runs
+# :app:checkDeniedDependencies, which resolves the release runtime classpath
+# and fails on every module whose group is listed here or sits below a listed
+# group. So com.google.firebase also covers com.google.firebase.crashlytics.
+#
+# Write each entry as "<group> # <reason>". The check rejects an entry without
+# a reason. Principle 1 in docs/PRD.md rules out telemetry, analytics, crash
+# reporting and ads. Section 8.1 says F-Droid accepts no proprietary libraries.
+
+# Proprietary Google libraries, which F-Droid's inclusion policy rejects.
+com.google.android.gms # Google Play Services, proprietary.
+com.google.firebase # Firebase, proprietary. Includes Analytics, Crashlytics and FCM push.
+com.google.android.play # Play Core libraries for in-app updates, reviews and Integrity. Proprietary and tied to the Play Store.
+com.google.android.datatransport # Uploads log events from Firebase SDKs to firebaselogging.googleapis.com.
+
+# Crash and error reporting.
+io.sentry # Sentry.
+com.bugsnag # Bugsnag.
+ch.acra # ACRA. Free software, but principle 1 rules out crash reporting.
+com.instabug.library # Instabug bug and crash reporting.
+com.microsoft.appcenter # Visual Studio App Center analytics and crash reporting.
+io.embrace # Embrace crash reporting and monitoring.
+
+# Analytics, monitoring and install attribution.
+com.datadoghq # Datadog monitoring, dd-sdk-android.
+com.newrelic.agent.android # New Relic mobile monitoring.
+io.opentelemetry.android # OpenTelemetry's Android agent. Free software, but it sends telemetry.
+com.amplitude # Amplitude analytics.
+com.mixpanel.android # Mixpanel analytics.
+com.segment.analytics # Segment analytics. Covers the Java SDK group and the Kotlin SDK group below it.
+com.flurry.android # Flurry analytics and ads.
+com.posthog # PostHog analytics.
+com.heapanalytics.android # Heap analytics.
+ly.count.android # Countly analytics.
+com.facebook.android # Facebook SDK. Its core module logs app events to Facebook.
+com.appsflyer # AppsFlyer install attribution.
+com.adjust.sdk # Adjust install attribution.
+io.branch.sdk.android # Branch deep linking and attribution.
+
+# Ads.
+com.applovin # AppLovin ads.
+com.unity3d.ads # Unity Ads.

+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+# Runs the instrumented tests against a real Tokkl server, never a fake one
+# (docs/PRD.md section 7).
+#
+# 1. Starts ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl, pinned by digest, on a free port of
+#    127.0.0.1 with its database on a tmpfs, and waits until it answers.
[credential-related line omitted]
+#    reads its API token from GET /api/v9/me.
+# 3. Runs connectedDebugAndroidTest with the server URL and the credentials as
+#    instrumentation arguments. The emulator reaches the host's 127.0.0.1 as
+#    10.0.2.2. The credentials work only on this container.
+# 4. Removes the container, also when a step fails.
+#
+# The tests run on the devices adb lists, or on ANDROID_SERIAL. With no device
+# attached, the script boots the AVD named by TOKKL_TEST_AVD (default
+# tokkl-api36) without a window and stops it at the end. It also stops the adb
+# server if that was not running before.
+#
+# Arguments go to Gradle, for example to run one class:
+#   scripts/instrumented-tests.sh \
+#     -Pandroid.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments.class=com.github.e0ipso.tokkl.api.ServerSmokeTest
+
+set -euo pipefail
+
+# Tokkl 1.3.1. To upgrade, take the index digest from
+# `docker buildx imagetools inspect ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl:<version>`.
+readonly IMAGE='ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl:1.3.1@sha256:12591e2b04d17cc6a12a7ff6bd0dffc718079797f73750d00695ef9ad916f9f9'
+readonly AVD="${TOKKL_TEST_AVD:-tokkl-api36}"
+readonly EMAIL='instrumented-tests@example.com'
+readonly RUNNER_ARGS='android.testInstrumentationRunnerArguments'
+
+cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
+
+for tool in docker adb curl jq; do
+    command -v "$tool" >/dev/null || { echo "$0: $tool is not on PATH" >&2; exit 1; }
+done
+
+work_dir=$(mktemp -d)
+container=''
+emulator_pid=''
+emulator_serial=''
+adb_was_running=false
+if pgrep -x adb >/dev/null; then adb_was_running=true; fi
+
+cleanup() {
+    local status=$?
+    if [ -n "$container" ]; then
+        docker rm --force --volumes "$container" >/dev/null 2>&1 ||
+            echo "$0: could not remove container $container" >&2
+    fi
+    if [ -n "$emulator_pid" ]; then
+        adb -s "$emulator_serial" emu kill >/dev/null 2>&1 || kill "$emulator_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
+        wait "$emulator_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
+    fi
+    if [ "$adb_was_running" = false ]; then
+        adb kill-server >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
+    fi
+    rm -rf "$work_dir"
+    exit "$status"
+}
+trap cleanup EXIT
+trap 'exit 130' INT
+trap 'exit 143' TERM
+
+random_hex() {
+    od -An -N"$1" -tx1 /dev/urandom | tr -d ' \n'
+}
+
+attached_devices() {
+    adb devices | awk 'NR > 1 && $2 == "device" { print $1 }'
+}
+
+boot_emulator() {
+    command -v emulator >/dev/null || { echo "$0: no device attached and emulator is not on PATH" >&2; exit 1; }
+    local port=5554 log="$work_dir/emulator.log" deadline=$((SECONDS + 300))
+    while adb devices | grep -q "^emulator-$port[[:space:]]"; do port=$((port + 2)); done
+    emulator_serial="emulator-$port"
+    echo "Booting $AVD as $emulator_serial"
+    emulator -avd "$AVD" -port "$port" -no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot -accel on >"$log" 2>&1 &
+    emulator_pid=$!
+    export ANDROID_SERIAL="$emulator_serial"
+    until [ "$(adb -s "$emulator_serial" shell getprop sys.boot_completed 2>/dev/null | tr -d '\r')" = 1 ]; do
+        if ! kill -0 "$emulator_pid" 2>/dev/null || [ "$SECONDS" -ge "$deadline" ]; then
+            echo "$0: $AVD did not boot. Last emulator output:" >&2
+            tail -n 20 "$log" >&2
+            exit 1
+        fi
+        sleep 2
+    done
+}
+
+if [ -z "${ANDROID_SERIAL:-}" ] && [ -z "$(attached_devices)" ]; then
+    boot_emulator
+fi
+

+  test('package.json declares every package the config imports (TKA-0083)', () => {
+    const source = readFileSync(join(repoRoot, 'commitlint.config.js'), 'utf8');
+    const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(repoRoot, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));

const aiTools = [
  'aider',
  'amp',
  'anthropic',
  'chatgpt',
  'claude',
  'codex',
  'copilot',
  'cursor',
  'devin',
  'gemini',
  'jules',
  'openai',
  'opencode',
  'qwen',
  'windsurf',
].join('|');

// Unicode-aware word boundaries, so "Ampère" or "OpenCoder" don't match.
const tool = `(?<![\\p{L}\\p{N}])(?:${aiTools})(?![\\p{L}\\p{N}])`;

const forbiddenPatterns = [
  // Any co-author, human or not.
  /^Co-authored-by:.*/im,
  // Made-with: Cursor, Assisted-by: Claude:claude-opus-5-5, ...
  new RegExp(`^(?:Assisted-by|Generated-by|Made-with|Signed-off-by):.*${tool}`, 'imu'),
  // Tool-specific trailers: Copilot-Session: ..., Amp-Thread: ...
  new RegExp(`^${tool}-[\\w-]+:`, 'imu'),
  // 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
  new RegExp(`(?:Generated|Made) (?:with|by|using)\\b.*${tool}`, 'iu'),
];

// TKA is this repo's prefix. TT is Tokkl's, kept from the copied rule.
const ticketIdInHeader = /\b(?:TKA|TT)-\d+\b/;

const findForbiddenContent = raw => {
  for (const pattern of forbiddenPatterns) {
    const match = raw.match(pattern);
    if (match) {
      return match[0];
    }
  }

  return null;
};

const findTicketId = header => (header ?? '').match(ticketIdInHeader)?.[0] ?? null;

// commitlint skips every rule for a message that matches one of its default ignores: merge
// commits, `Revert "..."`, `fixup!`/`squash!`, a bare version number and a few more. That
// also skipped the two custom rules below, so a revert or fixup could carry a ticket ID or a
// credit trailer. Turn the defaults off and apply them again only to messages that pass both
// custom checks. A message that fails one goes through the full rule set and is rejected.
const passesCustomChecks = message =>
  findForbiddenContent(message) === null && findTicketId(message.trimStart().split(/\r?\n/, 1)[0]) === null;

// The merge defaults match a "Merge branch ..." line anywhere in a message, so `added a thing`
// with that line in its body skipped the format rules. Hand the defaults the first line only.
// The other defaults already look at the start of the message, so they see no change.
const firstLine = message => message.split(/\r?\n/, 1)[0];

const ignoreCleanDefaults = (message = '') => isIgnored(firstLine(message)) && passesCustomChecks(message);

export default {
  extends: ['@commitlint/config-conventional'],
  defaultIgnores: false,
  ignores: [ignoreCleanDefaults],
  plugins: [
    {
      rules: {
        'no-ai-generated': parsed => {
          const match = findForbiddenContent(parsed.raw);
          if (match) {
            return [false, `Commit message contains forbidden AI-generated content: "${match}"`];
          }

          return [true, ''];
        },
        'no-ticket-id-in-header': parsed

PATH ../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts EXISTS True
import type { DurationFormat } from '@tokkl/types';

const UNIT_SECONDS: Record<string, number> = {
  h: 3600, hr: 3600, hrs: 3600, hour: 3600, hours: 3600,
  m: 60, min: 60, mins: 60, minute: 60, minutes: 60,
  s: 1, sec: 1, secs: 1, second: 1, seconds: 1,
};

/**
 * Parses what a person types into a duration field and returns whole seconds.
 *
 * Accepted: "1:30" (h:mm), "1:30:15" (h:mm:ss), "90" (minutes), "1.5" or "1,5h"
 * (decimal hours when a unit or separator says so), "1h 30m", "1h30m", "45s".
 * Returns null for anything else.
 */
export function parseDuration(raw: string): number | null {
  const input = raw.trim().toLowerCase().replace(',', '.');
  if (input === '') return null;

  const colon = /^(\d+):(\d{1,2})(?::(\d{1,2}))?$/.exec(input);
  if (colon) {
    const [, h, m, s] = colon;
    if (Number(m) > 59 || Number(s ?? 0) > 59) return null;
    return Number(h) * 3600 + Number(m) * 60 + Number(s ?? 0);
  }

  // A bare integer means minutes, a bare decimal means hours.
  if (/^\d+$/.test(input)) return Number(input) * 60;
  if (/^\d*\.\d+$/.test(input)) return Math.round(Number(input) * 3600);

  const unitPattern = /(\d+(?:\.\d+)?|\.\d+)\s*([a-z]+)/g;
  let total = 0;
  let consumed = '';
  for (const match of input.matchAll(unitPattern)) {
    const seconds = UNIT_SECONDS[match[2]!];
    if (seconds === undefined) return null;
    total += Number(match[1]) * seconds;
    consumed += match[0];
  }
  if (consumed.replace(/\s+/g, '') !== input.replace(/\s+/g, '')) return null;
  return Math.round(total);
}

function pad(n: number): string {
  return String(n).padStart(2, '0');
}

export function formatDuration(totalSeconds: number, format: DurationFormat = 'improved'): string {
  const seconds = Math.max(0, Math.floor(totalSeconds));
  if (format === 'decimal') return `${(seconds / 3600).toFixed(2)} h`;

  const h = Math.floor(seconds / 3600);
  const m = Math.floor((seconds % 3600) / 60);
  const s = seconds % 60;
  if (format === 'classic') {
    if (h > 0) return `${h}:${pad(m)}:${pad(s)}`;
    if (m > 0) return `${m}:${pad(s)} min`;
    return `${s} sec`;
  }
  return `${h}:${pad(m)}:${pad(s)}`;
}

/** Compact form for totals and chart labels, e.g. "12h 05m". */
export function formatHoursMinutes(totalSeconds: number): string {
  const minutes = Math.floor(Math.max(0, totalSeconds) / 60);
  const h = Math.floor(minutes / 60);
  const m = minutes % 60;
  return h > 0 ? `${h}h ${pad(m)}m` : `${m}m`;
}

PATH ../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts EXISTS True
const TIME_OF_DAY = /^(\d{1,2})(?:[:.]?(\d{2}))?(?::\d{2})?(a|am|p|pm)?$/;

function matchTimeOfDay(raw: string): RegExpExecArray | null {
  return TIME_OF_DAY.exec(raw.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, ''));
}

/**
 * Parses a typed clock time into minutes after midnight.
 *
 * Accepted: "9", "930", "0930", "9:30", "9.30", "9:30:15" (seconds dropped), "9a", "9pm", "12am".
 * Without a suffix, 24-hour input is taken literally. For 12-hour users, AM or PM for 1-11 is guessed
 * like Toggl does: with `after` (an end time), the first of the two that follows `after`, wrapping past
 * midnight; otherwise whichever is closer to `reference` (a start time, guessed against now).
 * Times are minutes after midnight. An explicit suffix is never second-guessed.
 */
export function parseTimeOfDay(
  raw: string, options: { twelveHour?: boolean; reference?: number; after?: number } = {},
): number | null {
  const match = matchTimeOfDay(raw);
  if (!match) return null;
  let hour = Number(match[1]);
  const minute = Number(match[2] ?? 0);
  const suffix = match[3];
  if (minute > 59) return null;

  if (suffix) {
    if (hour < 1 || hour > 12) return null;
    if (suffix.startsWith('a')) hour = hour === 12 ? 0 : hour;
    else hour = hour === 12 ? 12 : hour + 12;
    return hour * 60 + minute;
  }
  if (hour > 23) return null;
  if (options.twelveHour && hour >= 1 && hour <= 11) {
    const am = hour * 60 + minute;
    const pm = am + 12 * 60;
    const { after } = options;
    if (after !== undefined) {
      const wait = (candidate: number) => (candidate - after + 24 * 60) % (24 * 60);
      return wait(pm) < wait(am) ? pm : am;
    }
    if (options.reference !== undefined) {
      return Math.abs(pm - options.reference) < Math.abs(am - options.reference) ? pm : am;
    }
  }
  return hour * 60 + minute;
}

test('parseDuration accepts the formats people type', () => {
  const cases: Array<[string, number | null]> = [
    ['1:30', 5400],
    ['0:05:30', 330],
    ['90', 5400],
    ['1.5', 5400],
    ['1,5h', 5400],
    ['1h 30m', 5400],
    ['1h30m', 5400],
    ['2 hours 15 min', 8100],
    ['45s', 45],
    ['', null],
    ['abc', null],
    ['1:75', null],
    ['1x', null],
    ['1h foo', null],
  ];
  for (const [input, expected] of cases) {
    assert.equal(parseDuration(input), expected, `input ${JSON.stringify(input)}`);
  }
});

  assert.equal(formatDuration(306, 'improved'), '0:05:06');
  assert.equal(formatDuration(306, 'classic'), '5:06 min');
  assert.equal(formatDuration(6, 'classic'), '6 sec');
  assert.equal(formatDuration(3906, 'classic'), '1:05:06');
  assert.equal(formatDuration(2826, 'decimal'), '0.79 h');
  assert.equal(formatDuration(-5, 'improved'), '0:00:00');

44: - Fixing a forgotten or wrong entry.
45: - Checking this week's or month's totals.
46: - Stretches with no route to the server, such as away from home with the VPN down.
47: 
48: ## 3. Backends
49: 
50: Known from the tokkl repo (`../tokkl/docs/PRD.md` and source, read 2026-10-17):
51: 
52: - Tokkl implements a subset of the Toggl Track API v9 at `https://<server>/api/v9` and of the Reports API v3 at `https://<server>/reports/api/v3`. A client that accepts a base URL needs no other change to talk to it.
[credential-related line omitted]
54: - Tokkl adds two fields to `GET /api/v9/me`: `role` and `time_resolution_minutes`. Stopped entries snap to that resolution on the server.
55: - Some web app features use Tokkl-only endpoints outside the Toggl API: the trash (`GET/DELETE /api/app/trash`, `DELETE /api/app/trash/{id}`) and undoing a delete (`POST /api/app/time_entries/{id}/restore`). Favorites are a Tokkl key in `/me/preferences`, up to 9 of them.
[credential-related line omitted]
57: - The Tokkl server listens on plain HTTP (`app.listen` in `packages/api/src/index.ts`). HTTPS comes from a reverse proxy or `tailscale serve` in front of it.
58: - Toggl Track's API lives at `api.track.toggl.com`.
59: 
60: Decisions:
61: 
62: - The first version targets Tokkl only. Toggl Track comes later by pointing the app at Toggl's base URL, because both speak the same API. The Toggl version hides the Tokkl-only features above.
63: - The app has no sync engine. The server is the source of truth. The phone keeps a cache of what it last loaded and a queue of changes it has not sent yet (section 5.9).
64: - The app makes few requests (principle 3), so that the base URL change works later within Toggl's quotas (risk 2).
65: 
66: ## 4. Principles
67: 
68: 1. **Data stays between the phone and the server.** No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting or ads, and no request to any host other than the configured server. This carries Tokkl's first principle over and matches F-Droid's rules.
69: 2. **Offline first.** Tapping Start or Stop never fails because the server is out of reach. The change is kept on the phone and sent later.
70: 3. **Few requests.** The app refreshes on events (app opened, timer changed, network back), not on a short polling timer. The running clock ticks on the phone from the start time.
71: 4. **Tokkl behavior.** Same time parsing, favorites, grouping and undo as the web app, in the account's timezone, so moving between the laptop and the phone needs no relearning.
72: 5. **Loud look, plain function.** Tokkl's sixth principle applies as written: any style that costs a readable number, a reachable control or a passing test gets dropped.
73: 
74: ### Visual direction
75: 
76: - **Components.** Material 3 through Compose Material 3, the official Material toolkit for Jetpack Compose.
77: - **Colour.** Always Tokkl's palette, never the wallpaper. Light theme: sand background, ink text, sunset orange primary, aqua secondary, hot pink and lime for states. Dark theme: navy background with the neon counterparts. The source of truth is the colour tokens in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/styles.css`. Dynamic color is off, at most an opt-in setting.
78: - **Type.** Anybody for the running clock, totals and headings, bundled with the app. Tokkl already ships it under the SIL Open Font License 1.1 (`../tokkl/packages/web/public/fonts/OFL.txt`). Everything else uses the system font.
79: - **Contrast and signals.** WCAG AA, as in Tokkl: 4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for non-text elements. Colour is never the only signal for a state.
80: - **Devices.** Phones first. Layouts adapt to tablets and foldables, with no separate design.
81: 
82: ## 5. Functional requirements
83: 
84: The first release is feature complete: it ships every requirement below, so they carry no priority. Every row is Planned.
85: 
86: ### 5.1 Server and sign-in
87: 
88: | ID | Requirement |
89: |---|---|
[credential-related line omitted]
91: | S2 | Accept HTTPS with a publicly trusted certificate or with a CA the user installed in Android. |
92: | S3 | Accept HTTPS with a self-signed certificate after the user reads a disclaimer and trusts it. The app shows the certificate's SHA-256 fingerprint, trusts that certificate only, and asks again when it changes. It never accepts any certificate without a check. |
93: | S4 | Accept plain `http://` after a warning, stronger than the one in S3, that the token travels unencrypted unless the network encrypts it (Tailscale does). |
94: | S5 | Keep the token encrypted with a key in the Android Keystore. |
95: | S6 | One server and one account at a time. Signing out warns when unsent changes would be lost, then clears the cache and the queue. |
96: | S7 | Read the timezone, first day of the week and `time_resolution_minutes` from `GET /api/v9/me`, and use the account timezone everywhere. |
97: | S8 | No Android backup. The manifest sets `android:allowBackup="false"`, and `dataExtractionRules` exclude everything from `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`, because on Android 12 and later `allowBackup="false"` alone does not stop device-to-device transfer on some phones ([Auto Backup](https://developer.android.com/identity/data/autobackup)). Keystore keys do not move to a new phone, so a restored token could not be decrypted, and a restored queue could replay old changes. On a new phone the user signs in again. |
98: 
99: ### 5.2 Timer
100: 
101: | ID | Requirement |
102: |---|---|
103: | T1 | Start and stop the timer, and edit the running entry: description, project, task, tags, billable. |
104: | T2 | Continue a past entry. |
105: | T3 | Show stopped entries as the server returns them, after it snaps them to `time_resolution_minutes`. Entries not yet sent are snapped on the phone with a port of `snapSpan` (`../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts`) in the account timezone, so their times do not change when the server answers. The running clock is not snapped. |
183: ## 6. Architecture
184: 
185: Proposed, except Kotlin and Jetpack Compose, which the owner confirmed. Nothing here is built.
186: 
187: ### 6.1 Stack
188: 
189: - Kotlin and Jetpack Compose with Compose Material 3. Android has been Kotlin-first since Google I/O 2019, and Compose is not available from Java ([Android's Kotlin-first approach](https://developer.android.com/kotlin/first)).
190: - Jetpack Glance for the widget. It builds home screen widgets from composables on the Compose runtime ([Glance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance)).
191: - A `TileService` for the Quick Settings tile.
192: - Room (SQLite) for the cache and the queue of unsent changes.
193: - WorkManager to send the queue when the network comes back.
194: - OkHttp and kotlinx.serialization for the API, with a trust manager that adds the pinned self-signed certificate (S3).
195: - A Kotlin port of three TypeScript functions, each checked against the cases in its test file: `parseDuration` in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.ts` (cases in `../tokkl/packages/common/duration.test.ts`), `parseTimeOfDay` in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.ts` (cases in `../tokkl/packages/web/src/lib/timeOfDay.test.ts`) and `snapSpan` (T3) in `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.ts` (cases in `../tokkl/packages/common/snap.test.ts`). The time-of-day parser lives in the web package, not in `packages/common`.
196: 
197: Every dependency must be free software (section 8.1).
198: 
199: ### 6.2 Network security
200: 
216: | Privacy | No network traffic to any host but the configured server. |
217: | Battery | No wakeups while no timer runs and the queue is empty. No short-interval polling. While a timer runs, one check every 15 minutes (section 6.3). |
218: | Accessibility | WCAG AA contrast, TalkBack labels on every control, colour never the only signal, layouts that survive the largest font scale. |
219: | Language | English. All text lives in string resources. No other language is planned; contributors can add translations. |
220: | Tests | JVM unit tests, Compose UI tests, and instrumented tests on an emulator against a real Tokkl server from the `ghcr.io/e0ipso/tokkl` image, never a fake one. |
221: 
222: ## 8. Distribution and release
223: 
224: ### 8.1 F-Droid
225: 
226: From F-Droid's docs, read 2026-10-17:
227: 
228: - The app must be free software under a free license, built by F-Droid from source with a free toolchain, with no proprietary libraries such as Google Play Services or Firebase ([Inclusion Policy](https://f-droid.org/docs/Inclusion_Policy/)).
229: - Store text and images live in the repo under `fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/`: `title.txt`, `short_description.txt` (80 characters at most), `full_description.txt` (4000 at most), `changelogs/<versionCode>.txt`, `images/icon.png` and `images/phoneScreenshots/` ([descriptions and graphics](https://f-droid.org/docs/All_About_Descriptions_Graphics_and_Screenshots/)).
230: - With `UpdateCheckMode: Tags`, F-Droid checks every git tag for the highest version code, reading it from the Gradle build by default ([build metadata](https://f-droid.org/docs/Build_Metadata_Reference/)).
231: - If the build is reproducible, F-Droid can publish the APK signed with the project's own key. `Binaries` points at a versioned APK URL, such as a GitHub release asset, and `AllowedAPKSigningKeys` holds the SHA-256 fingerprint of the signing certificate. F-Droid publishes only when its build matches ([reproducible builds](https://f-droid.org/docs/Reproducible_Builds/)). The docs ask for pinned build-tools and no embedded timestamps.
232: 
233: Decisions: the license is MIT, like Tokkl. The application ID is `com.github.e0ipso.tokkl`, the same ID as the macOS and KDE companions.
234: 
235: ### 8.2 GitHub and CI
236: 
237: The repo is local only for now. When it moves to `github.com/e0ipso/tokkl-android`, it copies Tokkl's setup:
238: 
239: - One `ci.yml` workflow on pull requests and pushes to `main`, with every action pinned to a commit SHA and its version in a comment, and Dependabot for updates.
240: - commitlint with Tokkl's rules: Conventional Commits, no AI or co-author trailers, no ticket IDs in the subject. A husky hook runs it locally.
241: - Jobs for the Gradle build, lint and unit tests, and for instrumented tests on an emulator. GitHub's Ubuntu runners need a udev rule before the emulator can use KVM ([android-emulator-runner](https://github.com/ReactiveCircus/android-emulator-runner)).
242: - semantic-release on pushes to `main` with Tokkl's release rules and `v${version}` tags. It writes `CHANGELOG.md` and the version into the Gradle build, derives `versionCode` from the version, and attaches the signed release APK to the GitHub release, where F-Droid's `Binaries` can find it.
243: - The signing key lives in GitHub Actions secrets, never in the repo.
244: 
245: ### 8.3 Versions and stores
246: 
247: - F-Droid only. No Play Store listing is planned. The signed APK on each GitHub release (section 8.2) serves anyone who sideloads.
248: - The app has its own version, independent of Tokkl's, with its own `v*` tags and changelog, starting at 0.1.0. semantic-release does not support a first release below 1.0.0 ([FAQ](https://semantic-release.gitbook.io/semantic-release/support/faq)), so the first release is a hand-made `v0.1.0` tag and semantic-release counts on from it. A commit marked as a breaking change then moves the app to 1.0.0.
249: - No minimum server version. The app checks what the server can do instead: a missing `time_resolution_minutes` in `GET /api/v9/me` means no snapping to show, and a 404 from a Tokkl-only endpoint such as `/api/app/trash` hides the features that use it. The same checks hide those features against Toggl later (risk 4).
250: 
251: ## 9. Risks and open questions

import semver from "semver";
const isSemver = (c) => {
    const firstLine = c.split("\n").shift();
    if (typeof firstLine !== "string") {
        return false;
    }
    const stripped = firstLine
        .replace(/^chore(\([^)]+\))?:/, "")
        .replace(/\[(skip|ci)(-|\s)(ci|skip)\]/i, "")
        .replace(/\((skip|ci)(-|\s)(ci|skip)\)/i, "")
        .trim();
    return semver.valid(stripped) !== null;

import { wildcards } from "./defaults.js";
export default function isIgnored(commit = "", opts = {}) {
    const ignores = typeof opts.ignores === "undefined" ? [] : opts.ignores;
    if (!Array.isArray(ignores)) {
        throw new Error(`ignores must be of type array, received ${ignores} of type ${typeof ignores}`);
    }
    const invalids = ignores.filter((c) => typeof c !== "function");
    if (invalids.length > 0) {
        throw new Error(`ignores must be array of type function, received items of type: ${invalids
            .map((i) => typeof i)
            .join(", ")}`);
    }
    const base = opts.defaults === false ? [] : wildcards;
    return [...base, ...ignores].some((w) => w(commit));
}

13: /**
14:  * Client for the Toggl Track API v9 and Reports API v3, as served by Tokkl or
15:  * by Toggl Track. Build endpoint URLs with [server], then call [get] or
16:  * [send]. Calls block the calling thread, so make them off the main thread.
17:  */
18: class TogglApiClient(
19:     val server: ServerUrl,
20:     private val credentials: ApiCredentials,
21:     private val httpClient: OkHttpClient = SharedHttpClient.instance,
22: ) {
23: 
24:     fun <T> get(url: HttpUrl, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
25:         send("GET", url, body = null, deserializer)
26: 
27:     /**
28:      * Sends [body] as JSON, or no body when it is null. OkHttp refuses a null
29:      * body for POST, PUT and PATCH.
30:      */
31:     fun <T> send(
32:         method: String,
33:         url: HttpUrl,
34:         body: JsonElement?,
35:         deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>,
36:     ): ApiResult<T> {
37:         val request = Request.Builder()
38:             .url(url)
[credential-related line omitted]
40:             .method(method, body?.let { ApiJson.encodeToString(JsonElement.serializer(), it).toRequestBody(JSON) })
41:             .build()
42:         return try {
43:             httpClient.newCall(request).execute().use { it.toResult(deserializer) }
44:         } catch (e: IOException) {
45:             ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.Network(e))
46:         }
47:     }
48: 
49:     private fun <T> Response.toResult(deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
50:         when {
51:             isSuccessful -> decode(body.string(), deserializer)
52:             code == 403 -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.AuthFailed)
53:             code == 429 -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.RateLimited(retryAfterSeconds(header("Retry-After"))))
54:             else -> ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.UnexpectedStatus(code))
55:         }
56: 
57:     private fun <T> decode(text: String, deserializer: DeserializationStrategy<T>): ApiResult<T> =
58:         try {
59:             ApiResult.Success(ApiJson.decodeFromString(deserializer, text))
60:         } catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {
61:             // kotlinx.serialization's SerializationException is an IllegalArgumentException.
62:             ApiResult.Failure(ApiError.InvalidResponse(e))
63:         }
64: 
65:     private companion object {
66:         val JSON = "application/json; charset=utf-8".toMediaType()
67: 
68:         /** Tokkl sends whole seconds. An HTTP date or a negative number counts as missing. */
69:         fun retryAfterSeconds(value: String?): Long? = value?.trim()?.toLongOrNull()?.takeIf { it >= 0 }
70:     }

    test('accepts a Conventional Commit with ticket IDs in a Refs trailer', async () => {
      for (const message of [
        'feat: add x\n\nRefs: TKA-0001',
        'docs: record x\n\nExplain why x matters.\n\nRefs: TKA-0001, TKA-0002',
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.deepEqual(report.errors, [], message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, true, message);
      }
    });
  });

  describe("messages commitlint ignores by default (TKA-0078)", () => {
    test('rejects a revert or fixup with a ticket ID in the header and a credit trailer', async () => {
      for (const message of [
        'Revert "feat: implement TKA-0001"\n\nCo-Authored-By: Jane <jane@example.com>',
        'fixup! feat: implement TKA-0001\n\nGenerated-by: Claude',
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, false, message);
        const rules = failedRules(report);
        assert.ok(rules.includes('no-ticket-id-in-header'), `${message}: ${rules}`);
        assert.ok(rules.includes('no-ai-generated'), `${message}: ${rules}`);
      }
    });

    test('rejects a revert, squash or merge that fails only one custom check', async () => {
      for (const [message, rule] of [
        ['Revert "feat: implement TKA-0001"', 'no-ticket-id-in-header'],
        ['squash! feat: add x\n\nCo-Authored-By: Jane <jane@example.com>', 'no-ai-generated'],
        ["Merge branch 'TKA-0001-x'", 'no-ticket-id-in-header'],
        ["Merge branch 'topic'\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)", 'no-ai-generated'],
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, false, message);
        assert.ok(failedRules(report).includes(rule), `${message}: ${failedRules(report)}`);
      }
    });

    test('accepts a clean revert with a Refs trailer', async () => {
      for (const message of [
        'Revert "feat: add x"\n\nRefs: TKA-0001',
        'Revert "feat: add x"\n\nThis reverts commit 0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef01234567.\n\nRefs: TKA-0001',
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.deepEqual(report.errors, [], message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, true, message);
      }
    });

    test('accepts clean fixup! and squash! messages', async () => {
      for (const message of ['fixup! feat: add x', 'squash! feat: add x\n\nFold the retry into the first commit.']) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.deepEqual(report.errors, [], message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, true, message);
      }
    });
  });

  describe('merge subjects count only in the header (TKA-0084)', () => {
    test('rejects a non-Conventional message with a merge line in the body', async () => {
      for (const message of [
        'added a thing\n\nMerge branch y',
        'added a thing\r\n\r\nMerge branch y',
        'added a thing\n\nMerge pull request #1 from a/b',
        "added a thing\n\nMerge tag 'v1.0'",
        'added a thing\n\nMerge x into y',
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.equal(report.valid, false, JSON.stringify(message));
        const rules = failedRules(report);
        assert.ok(rules.includes('type-empty'), `${JSON.stringify(message)}: ${rules}`);
        assert.ok(rules.includes('subject-empty'), `${JSON.stringify(message)}: ${rules}`);
      }
    });

    test('still accepts a message whose header is a merge subject', async () => {
      for (const message of [
        "Merge branch 'topic'",
        "Merge branch 'topic'\n\n* topic:\n  fix: handle z\n  feat: add z",
        "Merge branch 'topic'\r\n\r\n# Conflicts:\r\n#\tfile.txt",
        'Merge pull request #1 from a/b\n\nAdd the thing',
        "Merge tag 'v1.0'",
        "Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/main'",
        'Merge topic into main',
      ]) {
        const report = await lintMessage(message);
        assert.deepEqual(report.errors, [], JSON.stringify(message));
        assert.equal(report.valid, true, JSON.stringify(message));
      }
    });
  });
});

describe('commit-msg hook in a throwaway repository', () => {
  let fixture;

  // Drop every GIT_* variable so a run from inside a git hook cannot reach this checkout,
  // and HUSKY so a HUSKY=0 in the caller's shell cannot turn the hook off.
  const env = {
    ...Object.fromEntries(
      Object.entries(process.env).filter(([name]) => !name.startsWith('GIT_') && name !== 'HUSKY'),
    ),
    GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL: '/dev/null',
    GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM: '1',

I found a concrete API issue: Tokkl returns HTTP 200 with an empty body after deleting a time entry, but the client JSON-decodes every successful response and reports that delete as InvalidResponse. An in-memory check with the pinned serialization library confirmed that even Unit decoding rejects the empty body. I’m applying the verification-before-completion skill for the final checks.

 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

for name in ['ic_launcher_foreground.png','ic_launcher_monochrome.png']:

  size=struct.unpack('>I',b[pos:pos+4])[0];kind=b[pos+4:pos+8];data=b[pos+8:pos+8+size];pos+=size+12
  if kind==b'IHDR':w,h,depth,color,*_=struct.unpack('>IIBBBBB',data)
  if kind==b'IDAT':packed+=data
 assert (depth,color)==(8,6)
